# Chapter Eight.

...is brought about, if one only adds and pours pigeon dung and child’s urine onto it (which growth is truly a sign of a living **vitality** <note>original: *vigor*</note> and effect). What then would hinder or prevent it, if from the mentioned two items or other such things, the **life-giving and generative spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus vivificus & generativus*</note> were extracted through **distillation** <note>original: *destillando*</note>, and the tincture of common gold (which is the true **gold-making seed** <note>original: *Semen aurificum*</note>) were extracted with it? Should the dead not be reawakened and made living again, as it were, through this?

Indeed, to say nothing here of the mercurial **waters of life** <note>original: *aquarum vitæ*</note>, in both dry and moist forms, with which the dead **body of common gold** <note>original: *Corpus auri vulgaris*</note> is resuscitated in manifold ways and returned, manifesting itself with living effects; thus may the dead gold be endowed and clothed with equal life-forces, if only it is skillfully **calcined** <note>reduced to powder through heat</note> with common lead—which is called a <term>Mother of Metals: a substance believed by alchemists to nurture and facilitate the growth or purification of other metals</term> by those knowledgeable in mining and by fire-artists <note>alchemists or metallurgists</note>—and **consequently** <note>original: *consequenter*</note> nourished, increased, and over- [flowed] by its **radical moisture** <note>original: *humido radicali*; the fundamental life-fluid of a substance</note> as its **first-born vital liquor** <note>original: *liquore vitali primogenito*</note>.

...out, such as silver. Where then does the sulfur or *brightness of the Sun* <note>original: *splendor Solis*; alchemists used "Sun" (Sol) to refer to gold and its essential spirit</note> go? The Ninth Chapter.

## CHAPTER IX.

->Concerning<-

# The **DISSOLUTION** or **OPENING** of the pores of the body, or of living gold, in the doubled Philosophical Mercury, for the release of its seed.

The text follows: Take ten parts of the doubled Philosophical Mercury <note>original: *Philosophischen Mercurii*; a specialized alchemical solvent, not common quicksilver, believed to have transformative powers</note> and one part of the mentioned gold or silver; place this in a vial <note>original: *Phiol*</note> so that two-thirds remain empty, and seal it hermetically <note>original: *hermeticè sigillirt*; to seal a glass vessel airtight by melting the neck closed, named after Hermes Trismegistus</note>.

Sendivogius <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist and philosopher</note>, Treatise 7: It is necessary that the pores of the bodies be opened, so that the **Seed** <note>original Latin: *Sperma*; here referring to the generative essence or "life force" within the metal</note> may be emitted; in its center lies the seed, which is air. When that enters the proper matrix <note>original Latin: *matricem*; the "womb" or vessel where the alchemical reaction is nurtured</note>, it is congealed, and it congeals that which it finds to be pure, or—as I think—mixed with the impure.

The Ninth Chapter.

### ADDITION.

Sendivogius writes in Treatise 7: *The gold-making seed is extracted in two ways: Gently and with violence.* <note>original Latin: *Semen aurificum dupliciter extrahitur: Suaviter & cum violentia*</note> Therefore, if one does not wish to separate the **gold-making seed** from the **body of gold** *by violence* <note>original: *per violentiam*</note>—by means of **Saturn** <note>the alchemical name for lead, often used in harsh, high-heat smelting to purify gold</note>, as was indicated previously—then let one remain with the text of this chapter. One should proceed, according to its contents, *gently and slowly* <note>original Latin: *suaviter & lentè*</note> with the **dry water** <note>original: *aqua sicca*; a paradoxical alchemical term for a substance that acts like a liquid but does not wet the hands, often a specialized mercury</note>, which has grown from Nature itself with little help from Art, according to the seventh chapter. Sendivogius called this crystalline dry water his other **Steel** <note>original: *Chalybem*; in alchemy, "Steel" often refers to a substance that acts as a "magnet" to attract solar or celestial influences</note>, where he writes at the end of Treatise 9 thus: *There is also another Steel, which is likened to this one (namely to the Metallic one, or Saturn, in its magnetic attractive power), created by Nature of its own accord. It knows how to elicit—from the rays of the (earthly) sun, with wonderful force and virtue—that which so many men have sought, and which is the beginning of our work.* (That is, the other principle or ingredient; for it takes the place of **Form** <note>referring to the Aristotelian idea that a thing is composed of Matter and its defining "Form"</note>).

Otherwise, for the rest, it appears as though a distinction should be maintained between the **soul of the sun** <note>original: *anima solis*; the internal spiritual essence of gold</note> (as it is commonly called) and the **seed of gold**, because Sendivogius, in the preface of his book, calls the *extraction of the soul* a *mere persuasion* <note>original Latin: *animæ extractionem... meram persuasionem*; suggesting that simply extracting the "soul" or color of gold is an illusion of success rather than true transformation</note>. However, the favorable reader should know that where these two named essences are not considered as one thing in their substance by Sendivo-

<meta>The text breaks mid-word "Sendivo-" to be continued as "Sendivogius" on the following page.</meta>

It discusses the "Steel of the Sages" as a magnetic principle that captures solar energy to initiate the alchemical work.</summary>

The Ninth Chapter. 65

...regarded by [Sen]divogius: yet it (the **Soul** <note>original: *anima*</note>) may, when arranged in an artistically wise manner, produce an effect equal to the **seed of gold** <note>original: *semini auri*</note>. However, care must be taken that the correct **method of extraction** <note>original: *modus extractionis*</note> of the aforementioned **Soul** <note>original: *animæ*</note> is hit upon, for such is to be understood in two ways. 

The one is common alchemical, sophistical, and deceitful, where such extracted **Sulfur** <note>original: *Sulphur*</note> or **Soul of the Sun** <note>original: *anima Solis*; "Sun" is the alchemical symbol for Gold</note> tints with instability in the test. **Paracelsus** <note>The famous Swiss physician and alchemist (1493–1541) who pioneered the use of chemicals and minerals in medicine</note> calls this a **tincture of color, not of virtue** <note>original: *tincturam coloris, non virtutis*; meaning it changes the visual appearance but not the essential medicinal or physical power of the metal</note>, and mentions similar things regarding the **Sulfur of Gold** <note>original: *Sulphure auri*</note> in the chapter or treatise on Metallic Sulfur. **Sendivogius** <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist and chemist known for his work on air and the "philosopher's stone"</note> now calls this **method** <note>original: *modum*</note> a **pompous and vain delusion of ostentation, which occurs not without the loss and detriment of time, labor, and expense** <note>original: *pomposam ac vanam ostentationis delusionem, quæ fit non sine temporis, laborum ac sumtuum jactura & detrimento*</note>. 

The other **method** <note>original: *modus*</note>, however, is founded upon the **Philosophical Way** <note>original: *viam Philosophicam*</note>, by which one tints one metal or another—with true permanence in the test in **all requirements, really into gold** <note>original: *in omnibus requisitis realiter ad Solem*; "Sol" or the Sun is the traditional name for gold in alchemy</note>—whether it happens with profit or with loss. For even if it brings no profit initially and so quickly, one can nevertheless direct it so that it may redundantly reward the Artist for his labor. Concerning this, **Sendivogius** now says: **He has the doors of nature open to investigate further and higher secrets, and to acquire them with God's blessing** <note>original: *Ille habet fores naturæ apertas ad ulteriora & altiora arcana investiganda, & cum DEI benedictione acquirenda*</note>, which I did not want to neglect mentioning here **in passing** <note>original: *obiter*</note>.

### On the EXTRACTION OF THE GOLD-MAKING SEED, as the form of the stone, which is its other part in its composition.

The text says further: Let it stand for three months in the **vaporous fire** <note>original: *vaporischen Feuer*; a gentle, moist heat, similar to a steam bath, used to slowly "open" the metal</note>; by this, the gold will not only be sufficiently opened, but will also give off a **ruby redness** <note>original: *rubinische Röhte*</note>. This must be carefully observed, for it is the **Philosophical Gold** <note>original: *Philosophische Gold*; the essential, spiritualized essence of gold rather than the common metal</note> and the gold's **true seed** <note>original: *rechter Samen*</note>, and the second piece or part of our **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of transmuting metals and granting long life</note>. # The Tenth Chapter.
<page-num>67</page-num>
<header>The Tenth Chapter.</header>

...or gold. For the **sperm** or **seed of gold** is its **quintessence** <term>quinta essentia; the "fifth essence," a highly purified and spiritualized form of matter beyond the four physical elements</term>, the **Elixir**, the most perfect decoction and digestion, or the **Balsam of Sulphur**.

**Treatise II.** Afterward, place it in our fire, and let it become a **dry liquor** <note>original: *liquor siccus*</note>. First, the earth (that is, the **dry water**) will be resolved into water, which is called the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Mercurius Philosophorum*; not common quicksilver, but the essential transformative solvent of alchemy</note>, and this water dissolves those bodies of the **Sun and Moon** <note>original: *Solis & Lunæ*; gold and silver</note>, and consumes them, so that nothing remains except a tenth part, along with one part; and this shall be the **seed** or the **metallic radical moisture**.

->ADDITION.<-

How to proceed with the **dry water** for the **extraction of the gold-making seed** <note>original: *extraction seminis aurifici*</note>, the text and the figure give clear and sufficient indication. But how the Philosophers went to work for the same purpose <note>original: *ad idem intentum*</note> with the moist **Menstrual Water** <note>original: *Menstrual Wasser*; a specialized solvent, named for its cyclical power of "birth" rather than literal blood</note> (the preparation of which was shown in the first addition to Chapter 7), I set down here thus:

Take <note>original: *R.*, the symbol for *recipe*</note> 10 parts of the **Menstruum** and 1 part of **Living Gold** <note>original: *Auri vivi*; gold that has been chemically prepared or "opened," not the common metal used in coins</note> (not common gold). Dissolve it in gentle heat, pour it off by **decanting** <note>original: *per inclinationem*</note>, and let it evaporate gently until it is about half a finger high. Then place it in the cold, and beautiful, transparent, deep yellow **crystals** will form. Take these out with a **wooden pair of tongs** <note>original: *hölzern Putzscheer*; wooden tools were used to prevent contaminating the "pure" crystals with common metals</note>, put them into a **glass vial** <note>original: *Phiolglas*</note>, seal it **hermetically** <note>original: *hermeticè*; airtight, usually by melting the glass tip of the vial</note>, and place it in an **athanor** <note>a specialized furnace designed to provide steady, continuous heat over long periods</note> at the **first degree** of heat for 40 days; then it will turn black. At the **second degree**, it becomes ashes, and the **Peacock’s Tail** <note>original: *cauda pavonis*; a stage in the alchemical process where the matter displays a shimmering variety of colors like a peacock's feathers</note> appears.

<header>The Eleventh Chapter.</header>
<meta>catchword: Sen-</meta>

In the **third degree** <term>tertio gradu; the third of four traditional levels of heat in an alchemical furnace</term>, the matter becomes snow-white; drive this until the redness appears, so that you will finally see a beautiful, transparent redness manifest itself in the glass when the flame reaches the **fourth degree** <term>quarto gradu; the highest level of heat, often associated with the final transformation</term>. Watch this carefully; break the glass with the careful application <note>original: *Bescheidenheit*; here meaning skillful or moderate use</note> of a glowing iron and keep the redness. For it is the true **Philosophical Gold** and the **metallic Seed of Gold**; if you have even a barleycorn's weight of it, you have enough for your whole life.

# The Eleventh Chapter.

Sendivogius <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), an influential alchemist known for his theories on the "food of life" found in the air.</note> at the end of the fifth treatise: He who knows how to congeal water with heat, and to join the spirit—the spiritual **seed of gold** <note>original Latin: *spirituale semen auri*</note>—with it, will certainly find a thing a thousand times more precious than gold and every other thing. Let him therefore effect the separation of the spirit (the prolific spirit of gold) from the water (which was mixed in partnership with that gold) so that it may rot <term>putrescat; putrefaction or "rotting" was considered the essential first stage of any new growth in alchemy</term> and the grain or seed may appear. Afterward, having rejected the dregs, let him bring back the spirit from on high—the **seed of gold** which has particularly risen and sublimated itself in the glass <note>original German: *semen auri, so sich besonders im Glaß erhebt/und sublimirt hat*</note>—into the water, and cause them to be joined together. For that conjunction will generate a branch unlike the parents in form.

Treatise 8. Every seed is of no consequence by itself if it is not placed, either by art or by nature, into its proper **womb** <term>matrix; the vessel or substance—often the "Philosophical Mercury"—intended to receive and nourish the alchemical seed</term>. And although this seed is in itself more noble than every creature, yet its womb is such that it causes the grain to rot, and [of the] pure pun- <note>The text cuts off mid-word; likely *purificat* (purifies) or *purgent* (cleanses).</note>

<vocab>Sendivogius, seed of gold, spirit, putrefaction, womb (matrix), alchemy</vocab>

It explains that the spiritual essence of gold must be separated, allowed to undergo a process of decay (putrefaction), and then reunited with its purified "water" or "womb" to generate the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>

...to congelation <term>congelation; the process of turning a liquid into a solid or semi-solid state in alchemy</term> through cause; furthermore, it nourishes and makes it grow by the color of its own body. <note>original Latin: *ai congelationem causatim, insuper colore corporis sui illud nutrit & crescere facit*</note>

Treatise 9. When the seed is placed in its matrix <term>matrix; the vessel or substance that acts as a "womb" for the alchemical process</term>, it purges it and makes it a thousand times more fit to bring forth the best fruits. <note>original Latin: *Cum semen imponitur in suam matricem, purgat illam, & facit millesies apti-onem ad pariendum optimos fructus.*</note>

### ADDITION.

In the same proportion, the Philosophers have also proceeded by the wet way <note>The "wet way" (*via humida*) involves liquid solvents and gentle heat, whereas the "dry way" uses high heat and crucibles.</note> as follows: *Take 1 grain of this seed.* <note>original Latin: *R. hujus feminis granum 1.*</note> Add to it 10 grains of the above-mentioned heavy Menstrual Water <term>Menstrual Water; an alchemical solvent, often referring to the "mercury of the philosophers," named for its life-giving and cyclic associations</term>, and they will soon embrace one another. *Seal it hermetically* <note>original Latin: *sigillirs hermeticè*; to seal a glass vessel perfectly air-tight by melting the neck</note>, and proceed *through the stages of the Athanor* <term>Athanor; a self-feeding furnace designed to maintain a steady, continuous heat over long periods</term> for 5 months, until the work reaches perfection.

# CHAPTER XII.

->Concerning the<-

## MATURATION OF THE WORK, UNTIL ITS perfection and completion.

<note>original Latin: *MATURATIONE OPERIS, USQUE AD ejus perfectionem & complementum*</note>

Finally, the text concludes with these words: Place this in an Athanor, the Philosophical Oven; let it proceed with gentle heat for 7 months, and then, by the grace of God, one will find that which has been sought until now.

Sendivogius *Treatise 10.* It is afterwards governed by a continuous fire for seven months, and sometimes ten, until our water consumes three and leaves one, and this in double. <note>original Latin: *Regitur postea igne continuo per menses septem, & interdum de- cem, donec aqua nostra consumat tres, & relin- quat unum, & hoc in duplo.*</note> Afterwards it is nourished by the milk of the earth <term>milk of the earth; a metaphor for a nourishing alchemical substance or refined solvent believed to sustain the growing "stone"</term>, or its fatness, which is born in the breasts of the earth, and it is preserved from putrefaction <term>putrefaction; the stage of decay or blackening necessary for rebirth in the alchemical process</term> by the Salt of nature; and thus is generated that infant of the second generation <term>infant of the second generation; a symbolic name for the Philosopher's Stone as it reaches a new level of maturity and power</term>. <note>original Latin: *Postea nutritur lacte terræ, vel pinguedine ejus, quæ nascitur in uberibus terræ, & conservatur à putrefactio-ne Sale naturæ & sic generatur ille infans se-cundæ generationis.*</note>

In the Epilogue: Take airs (namely, those condensed in the form of salt-nitre <term>salt-nitre; potassium nitrate, which alchemists believed captured the "vital air" or "universal spirit"</term>, for which reason he also calls it "our earth" in *Treatise 11*) 10 grains. Of living Gold <term>living Gold; "aurum vivum," or philosophical gold that has been "quickened" or purified for the Work</term> or living Moon <term>living Moon; "Luna viva," or philosophical silver</term> 1 grain. Put all these into your vessel, and cook this air especially, so that it may be water, and afterwards not water. <note>original Latin: *Recipe aëres (sc. condensati in forma salis nitri... ) grana X. Auri vivi vel Lunæ vivæ gr. 1. Hæc omnia impone vasi tuo, & coque aërem hunc imprimis, ut sit aqua, & postea non aqua.*</note> And a little after: Dissolve the congealed air in which you dissolve a tenth part of gold; seal this, and work with our fire, until the air is turned into powder, and once the salt of the world <term>salt of the world; the "Sal Mundi," a universal purifying agent sought by alchemists</term> is obtained, various colors will appear. <note>original Latin: *Solve con-gelatum aërem in quo solvas auri decimam par-tem, hoc sigilla, & igne nostro labora, donec aër vertatur in pulverem, & apparebunt habito mundi sale colores varii.*</note>

Treatise II. **Recipe.** <note>original Latin: *R.*, an abbreviation for *Recipe*, meaning "Take."</note> Take the water of salt nitre <term>salt nitre; a purified form of potassium nitrate often associated in alchemy with the "spirit of the world"</term> from our earth, and place into this that metallic radical moisture; then place it into the fire of putrefaction and generation. This fire should not be the same as the one you used in the first operation (the extraction of the seed of gold). Govern everything with great skill until colors appear like the peacock's tail <term>peacock's tail; a stage in the alchemical process where a shimmering variety of colors appears in the vessel, signaling that the matter is transforming</term>. Continue governing by digesting <note>original Latin: *digerendo*; a process of gentle, sustained heating</note> and do not let it weary you until the colors cease and a single green color appears throughout. And so proceed with the rest. 

When you see brown-colored ashes at the bottom and the water is almost red, open the vessel and moisten a feather; smear it on some iron. If it tints the iron, have ready the water of which we will speak later, and pour in as much of that water as the amount of raw air that entered the vessel. Cook it again with the previous fire until it tints the iron once more. This is as far as my experience has reached. 

That water, however, must be the Menstruum of the world <term>Menstruum of the world; a universal solvent or life-force believed to be drawn from the atmosphere or the "sphere of the moon"</term> from the sphere of the Moon, rectified so many times that you are able to calcine the sun <note>original Latin: *calcinare solem*; "to calcine the sun" means to reduce gold to a fine, chemically reactive powder</note>. 

Regarding the fire, it remains to be said: the fire is double. The first fire is continuous and surrounds the matter; it is the external fire that assists the internal. The second is the natural fire, which digests and fixes the matter. This fire of nature is one and the same in both the first and second operations; whatever different effects it produces are caused by the distance of the locations <note>this refers to the placement of the vessel within the furnace to regulate heat intensity</note>. A single vessel would even suffice, but for the sake of speed we use two; the vessel for the first work must be round. In

It explains the use of a "Menstruum of the world" to treat gold and defines the roles of external and internal fires in the transformation.</summary>

<header>The Twelfth Chapter. 73</header>

...and secondly, truly a little less in the shape of a vial or an egg. The material is also unique, but composed of two united substances. Thus, it is both worthless and most precious. <note>The "worthless and precious" paradox is a common alchemical trope describing the "First Matter," which was believed to be a common substance found everywhere but ignored by the ignorant.</note> Now I have revealed everything, as much in the first work as in the second.

**In a riddle:** Our water <note>original Latin: *Aqua nostra*</note> possesses an internal fire, and if it is assisted by continuous heat, it burns away three of its parts, and nothing will remain except a very minute part, which can hardly be imagined, but is of the highest power. It is cooked by the wise skill of the Master for seven months at first, then for ten. But meanwhile, various things appear, and always on the fiftieth day, more or less.

**In the same place:** It can be improved further, as the Philosophical Scripture <note>referring to the corpus of established alchemical writings</note> testifies; it can be exalted <note>refined to a higher state of potency</note> first tenfold, then a hundredfold, then a thousandfold and ten-thousandfold, and so on.

---

### ADDITION.

Finally follows how the Philosophers have multiplied <note>The process of "multiplication" increases the quantity and the transmuting power of the Alchemical Stone.</note> their work in the **wet way** <term>via humida; a path of alchemy using liquid solvents and gentle heat, often symbolized by the slow growth of a plant</term> (and without doubt also in the **dry way** <term>via sicca; a faster, high-heat path using crucibles and furnaces</term>). Therefore, the multiplication occurs both in quantity and in quality, always using ten parts of its water or Philosophical double Mercury <term>double Mercury; a refined alchemical substance believed to combine the "fixed" and "volatile" principles necessary for the Stone</term>, and thus it continues unto infinity. The first time, it does not tint <note>original German: *tingirt*; the act of the Stone changing the color and nature of a base metal into gold or silver</note>; however, when it is applied for the second time, then the...

<header># The Twelfth Chapter.</header>

...work is completed in two months and tinges <note>The term "tinge" refers to the alchemical transformation or transmutation of a base metal into a noble one.</note> only ten parts for every one part of the Stone. When set in for the third time and multiplied, it is finished in three weeks and tinges one part to a hundred parts. When set in for the fourth time, it is finished in three days and tinges a thousand parts. Hereafter, the work of Multiplication always finishes in three days, and this is the **Work of Three Days** <note>original Latin: *Opus trium dierum*; a famous alchemical stage where the process reaches its peak efficiency</note>. After the **Stone** <note>original Latin: *lapis*</note> is highly multiplied, it then also has the power **according to the quantity** <note>original Latin: *secundum quantitatem*</note> to tinge; and first, its first part is cast upon ten parts of pure melted gold, acting as the **ferment of ferments** <note>original Latin: *fermentum fermenta*; the substance that triggers the final transformation</note>. Thus it becomes a brittle, glassy medicine and a tincture **over imperfect bodies** <note>original Latin: *super imperfecta corpora*; "bodies" refers to base metals like lead or tin</note>, especially **Mercury** <note>original Latin: *Mercurium*</note>, as it is an "open" metal. However, as for the Stone itself, before it is mixed with the gold, it is to be used for the human body; its **dose** <note>original Latin: *dosis*</note> is one
->**grain**, in **any**<-
->**liquid whatsoever.**<- <note>original Latin: *granum, in quocunque liquore*. A "grain" was a tiny unit of measurement, roughly equivalent to the weight of a seed.</note>

<page-num>78</page-num>
<header># The Twelfth Chapter.</header>

->The following should be referred to the addition of the seventh chapter near its end.<- <note>original Latin: *Sequentia referantur ad additionem capitis septimi sub finem.*</note>

## The Second Addition.

Further, it is necessary here to introduce and mention the **dry crystalline water**, which grows and shoots out <note>In this context, "shooting out" refers to the process of crystallization, where crystals appear to grow rapidly out of a liquid solution.</note> from the **Oil of common Salt**, called by the artists **Sweet Salt Crystals** <term>original: *Cristalli salis dulces*; these were highly purified salt crystals used in both alchemy and early medicine</term>. Such a substance is well worthy of the name **Philosophical Mercury** <term>original: *Mercurii Philosophici*; not the liquid metal we know as quicksilver, but the essential "spirit" or transformative principle that alchemists believed was the soul of all matter</term>, and has always been held in great esteem as a portion of the **Philosophical Stone**. This is because common salt is established as the **center of the element of water** <note>original: *centrum elementi aquae*</note>, acting as the root and beginning of **metallic birth** <note>Alchemists believed that metals grew inside the earth from "seeds" or "roots" found in saline substances.</note>; this is dealt with more extensively in the introduction of this book.

As for how such dry salt-water is prepared and obtained, **Andreas de Platen** <note>A 16th-century physician and alchemist known for his chemical recipes.</note> has indeed described and indicated it **candidly and sincerely** <note>original: *ingenue & sincere*</note> in the **Letters of Mattioli** <note>Referring to the correspondence of Pietro Andrea Mattioli (1501–1577), a famous Italian physician and botanist.</note>. However, these crystals are much more excellent and effective when they are prepared from common salt that has first been well moistened several times with the **Soul of Saturn** <term>original: *anima Saturni*; in alchemy, Saturn is the code name for lead. Its "soul" or "essence" often refers to lead acetate or a specific tincture derived from lead</term> and dried again, and then distilled **according to the rules of the art** <note>original: *lege artis*; a common phrase meaning the procedure was performed following the established professional standards of the craft</note>. For by this, the **coldness of the highest metal** <note>original: *frigiditas metalli summi*; though gold is usually the "highest" metal, in certain chemical sequences, lead (Saturn) is considered the "highest" or "first" because it is the heaviest and the starting point of the work.</note> is imprinted upon the work, and thereby the crystallizing **saltpeter-like nature** is strengthened.

<meta>catchword: Lässestu (Thou lettest)</meta>

<summary>This page describes the preparation of "Sweet Salt Crystals" (Philosophical Mercury) from common salt, emphasizing its role as the root of metallic life and the influence of lead (Saturn) in its refinement. It credits the physician Andreas de Platen and refers to the works of Mattioli for the foundational recipe.</summary>

<page-num>76</page-num>
<header>The Twelfth Chapter.</header>

If you now allow the crystallization <note>original: *Anschiessung*; the process where crystals "shoot" or form out of a liquid</note> to occur by its own nature **under the open sky** <note>original: *sub dio*</note>, and permit it to be impregnated with the **astral influence** by means of the trapped air—following the process of the fifth, sixth, and seventh chapters—then you need not inquire after the labors of the second, third, and fourth chapters (which were accomplished simply from **bole earth** <note>a medicinal red clay</note>, without any addition). 

Through this, you receive a very noble **Magnesia of Fire** <term>Magnesiam ignis</term>, which, being nourished and increased in the fire, as well as before and with fire, gives birth to and brings forth the true **Son of Fire** <term>filium ignis</term>. If the addition of the **subject of earth** <note>the base material</note> then occurs to the **Animated Salt** <term>Sale animato</term>, and it is then distilled with **aqua regis** <note>original abbreviation: *e. a.*, likely referring to the solvent "royal water"</note> according to the method and governance of fire indicated by the aforementioned **de Platen**, then it is all the better.

Otherwise, to proceed further with the **sweet crystals of Oil of Salt**, prepared simply according to the common process, the practice of a high **Philosophical Work** has been extensively described in verse by **Johannes Augurellus** <note>Giovanni Aurelio Augurello (c. 1441–1524), an Italian humanist whose poem *Chrysopoeia* was a famous alchemical text</note>. This was afterwards condensed into a short compendium, to which I wish to direct the favorable **son of the art** <term>artis filium</term>. Concerning the final stage <note>original: *poster Projection*</note> of this work, the named author has playfully and hyperbolically alluded, saying: "If the whole sea were quicksilver, it would be transmuted into gold." <note>original Latin: *Quod si totum foret æquor Argentum vivum, transmutaretur in aurum.*</note> He adds the indication that such a work may not be completed in less than three years, and therefore, for the sake of a clear distinction from and...

It emphasizes that the true alchemical transformation is a long-term process, potentially taking three years to complete.</summary>

# The Twelfth Chapter.

is called the **Work of Three Years** <note>original: *Opus trium annorum*</note> above other philosophical works <note>original: *operibus*</note>. For because the practice <term>Practick</term> <note>the practical laboratory application of alchemical theory</note> is manifold—since there are **many ways toward one goal** <note>original: *multæ viæ ad unum intentum*</note>—and some works follow the short way, while others follow the long way, necessity requires us to properly distinguish these so-very-many works <note>original: *Opera*</note> in order to correctly understand the numerous different philosophical writings. One should not want to **strictly twist** <note>original: *strictè torquiren*; to force a narrow interpretation</note> each and every thing toward **only a single stone, a single medicine, a single vessel, a single fire, etc.** <note>original: *ad unicum tantùm lapidem, Medicinam unam, vas unum, ignem unum, &c.*</note>.

For although they all stem from one root and are considered one at their foundation—and must also be composed in their preparation <note>original: *Præparation*</note> from two ingredients <note>original: *Ingredienzen*</note>, namely **matter and form, the acting agent and the passive subject** <note>original: *Materia & forma, Agente & patiente*</note>—the practice of such a composition is nonetheless unequal, from which different works have arisen. 

Therefore, following the mentioned three-year work, others follow which are called the **Work of Three Months, Three Weeks, Three Days** <note>original: *Opus trium Mensium, Trium Septimanarum, Trium dierum*</note>. Among these, one surpasses all others: the **Work of Three Hours** <note>original: *Opus trium horarum*</note>, which **Mary the Prophetess** <note>also known as Miriam the Jewess, a legendary foundational figure in alchemy</note> possessed. From the dialogue <note>original: *Interlocution*</note> she held with the **Philosopher Aros**, this work can still be well-discerned by a keen-witted, profound, and well-practiced craftsman <note>original: *artifice*</note>. Conversely, there are some works called the **Work of one natural day, of one month, of one year**, likewise **of nine**

of months, etc. He who has not grasped a proper distinction between these and similar things might well say to himself in good time: **Hands off the slate** <note>original: *Manum de tabula*; a Latin idiom meaning to stop working or leave a task alone before one spoils it through over-refining</note>. And although the mentioned **works** <note>original: *opera*</note> are all to be understood only in terms of the duration of the **subsequent work** <term>Nacharbeit</term> <note>the second, more advanced phase of the alchemical process where the prepared matter is brought to perfection</note>: there may yet be several other works alongside or beneath them, both of whose operations—namely the **preliminary work** <term>Vorarbeit</term> <note>the initial stage of cleaning, distilling, or preparing raw materials</note> and the subsequent work—are comprised within a set time and goal. It is unnecessary to explain this at greater length; I only wanted to indicate it to you here **in passing** <note>original: *obiter*</note>.

### &
# RECAPITULATION
## OF THIS BOOK.

Although I have, in the preceding chapters, commented upon the text that was attached to these **symbolic plates** <note>original: *tabulis typicis*; these refer to the alchemical illustrations accompanying the text</note>—to the extent that God has granted me understanding through diligent reading and investigation, along with conversation and laboratory work <note>original: *laboriren*, referring to practical chemical experimentation</note>—and have thus shared my explanation in the manner of a **commentary** <note>original: *Commentarii vice*</note> in good and sincere faith with the **sons of Hermes** <note>original: *filiis Hermetis*, a traditional title for students of alchemy and followers of the legendary Hermes Trismegistus</note>: I have nevertheless deemed it proper to add a short appendix. In this, not only will the **preceding matters** <note>original: *præcedentia*</note> concerning this **Great Work** <note>The "Great Work" (*Magnum Opus*) is the alchemical process of creating the Philosopher's Stone or achieving spiritual transformation.</note> be repeated as a **summary** <note>original: *Summa*</note>, but any points that might have been overlooked will be carefully addressed and rectified.
<meta>catchword: But</meta>

<summary>In this opening to the book's epilogue, the author Orthelius explains his intention to summarize his commentary on alchemical plates and provide a final, corrected overview of the "Great Work" for his fellow alchemists.</summary>
<keywords>Epilogue, Recapitulation, alchemy, sons of Hermes, symbolic plates, Great Work, laboratory work, Orthelius</keywords>

--- Page 89 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Epilogue and Recapitulation." The catchword from the previous page was "But," which transitions into the opening sentence here, expanding the scope of the summary to include incidental knowledge alongside the "Great Work."</meta>

(2.)

F 2

But also of other things, which were mentioned incidentally and **in passing** <note>original: *obiter mention*</note>, even though they do not concern the previously mentioned **Principal Work** <note>The "Principal Work" or *Principal-Werck* refers to the core alchemical process of the *Magnum Opus*.</note>. This is done so that, for the sake of more delightful knowledge, a somewhat clearer and brighter understanding might follow; for as **Scaliger** <note>Julius Caesar Scaliger (1484–1558) was a highly influential Renaissance scholar and physician known for his works on grammar, poetics, and natural philosophy.</note> says:

> Nothing is more blessed than to know, nothing more divine than to teach, nothing closer to true happiness than to learn: and for a noble man, not only is every cessation [of effort] a form of idleness, but even becoming weary of seeking must be considered most shameful, especially when that which is sought is most beautiful. <note>original Latin: *Nihil est beatius, quàm scire, nihil divinius, quàm docere, nihil propius veræ fælicitati, quàm discere: omnisque non solùm cessatio ingenuo viro ignavia est ; sed etiam quærendi defatigatio existimari debet turpissima, ubi id , quod quæritur , est pulcherrimum.*</note>

Therefore, regarding what first and foremost concerns the often-mentioned **Main Work** of this book, it is indicated in the **Preface** <note>original: *Præfation*</note> how the **Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophi*; in an alchemical context, "Philosophers" refers to the "Adepts" or masters of the chemical arts.</note> who lived both in ancient times and in current **ages** <note>original: *Seculis*</note>, and who devoted themselves to the righteous and true **Alchemy** <note>original: *Alchimiæ*</note> with all earnestness and diligence, directed all their **intent** and labor toward this: that they might maintain their lives in the best health, and honestly support themselves without being a burden to their fellow man, as much from the abundance of their sustenance as with the **gift**... <note>The sentence ends mid-thought; the "gift" (Latin: *dono*) likely refers to the *donum Dei* or the Gift of God, a common term for the Philosopher's Stone.</note>

<meta>catchword: Medi-</meta>

<summary>Orthelius cites the scholar Scaliger to justify his pursuit of knowledge and explains that the true goal of ancient and modern alchemists was to maintain health and live honorably without burdening others.</summary>
<keywords>Scaliger, Alchemy, health, philosophers, Great Work, purpose of alchemy, lifelong learning</keywords>

**Medicine** <note>original: *Medicinæ*</note> so that they might serve and assist their needy and sick Christian brothers. To which **purpose** <note>original: *Scopo*</note>, after they had thoroughly surveyed the entire **university of nature** <note>original: *Universität der Natur*; here referring to the complete scope and breadth of the natural world rather than an academic institution</note>, no better or more useful means was found than that which they—on account of its translucent, diamond-like and ruby-like appearance—called the noble and blessed **Philosopher’s Stone**. **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the foundation of alchemy is attributed.</note>, however, in his **Emerald Tablet** <note>original: *Smaragdinischen Tafel*; a short, cryptic text highly revered by alchemists as the foundation of their art.</note>, called it the "Honor and Glory of the whole world" on account of its **effect** <note>original: *propter effectum*</note>. Others, his successors, have called it the **highest temporal and fleeting good**, as well as the **divinely given Pandora** <note>In this context, "Pandora" is used in its literal Greek sense of "all-gifted" or "giving all," rather than referring to the mythological box of evils.</note> and the **health-bringing Panacea** <note>original: *Bonum summum temporale & caducum, nec non divinitùs datam Pandoram & salutiferam Panacæam*</note>; and some, by way of comparison and not unjustly, have named it the **Earthly Savior** <note>original: *Salvatorem terrenam*</note>.

For just as (**And yet here, great things are not compared with small, but small things are compared with great in certain ways** <note>original Latin: *Neque tamen hic magna componuntur parvis, sed parva magnis in quibusdam modis comparantur*</note>) the supernatural and eternal good, our Mediator and Savior, **Jesus Christ**—who sets us loose and free from eternal death, the Devil, and all evil—partakes of two natures: **Divine and Human**; so consists

)3) 4. (&c

This **earthly Savior** <note>original: *Salvator*</note> also consists of two parts: heavenly and earthly; so that it may heal us and free us from heavenly and earthly, spiritual and physical, visible and invisible diseases. (I am not speaking here of the **diseases of the soul** <note>original: *de morbis animæ*</note>, as these are beyond nature and therefore require the **supernatural Savior and Physician** <note>original: *supernaturalis Salvatoris & Medici*</note>; nor am I speaking of those **natural ailments in which something divine intervenes** <note>original: *naturalibus, quibus aliquid divini incurrit*</note>, or which through neglect and abandonment have become entirely **incurable** <note>original: *immedicabilisch*</note>; but rather I shall speak of these elsewhere). It takes its **earthly principles** <note>original: *Principia*</note> (as the theory of this book contains, and as the practice is clearly enough depicted in figures) partly from common **red clay or loam** <note>original: *rothen Thon oder Letten*</note>; and partly from the metal **Gold**, which is the noblest **subject** <note>original: *Subjectũ*</note>, **as much for the medicine of man as for the transmutation of metals** <note>original: *tam ad medicinam hominis, quàm ad transmutationem metallorũ*</note>. Its **heavenly parts** <note>original: *Partes*</note> or principles, however, it takes by means of **atmospheric water** <note>original: *Lufftwasser*; likely referring to dew or rainwater, believed by alchemists to contain celestial vitality</note> from the **celestial rays of the Sun and Moon** <note>original: *radiis cœlestibus Solis & Lunæ*</note>; which rays, spiritual though they may be, are nonetheless considered by the **philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophis*; meaning the alchemists or "natural philosophers"</note>...

This is what constitutes the **"above" and "below" of Hermes** <note>original: *Hermetis superius ac inferius*; a reference to the Hermetic principle "As above, so below"</note>, whether it exists in a **hidden or manifest** <note>original: *occulto vel manifesto*</note> state, or in **actuality or potentiality** <note>original: *actu vel potentiâ*</note>, as will be more fully understood from the attached writings that follow.

Furthermore: just as Christ was appointed by GOD as a sign that shall be spoken against by almost everyone—for which reason he is called in Divine Holy Scripture a **stumbling block** <note>original: *Lapis offensionis*</note>, as well as the **cornerstone** <note>original: *Eckstein*</note> which the builders rejected—in the very same way, it happens also to the **Stone of the Wise** <note>original: *Lapidi Sophorum*</note>. For just as many heretics and sects, and even **false Christs** <note>original: *Pseudo-Christi*</note> themselves, arise and have arisen, just as many and more are found here who either entirely contradict this **Stone** <note>original: *Lapidi*</note>, mocking, deriding, nullifying, and rejecting it; or yet

yet devise and put forward **false materials** <note>original: *Pseudo-materias*</note> from which they think and teach others to make this highest earthly **treasure of nature** <note>original: *Schatz der Natur*</note>, in a manner entirely contrary to nature. But just as those are not to be believed who say: "Here is **Christ**": so too should one give no credence to these **philosophasters** <note>original: *Philosophastris*; a derogatory term for those who pretend to be philosophers but lack true knowledge</note> when they say: "this is the **material** <note>original: *materia*</note>, that is the **material**" of the **Universal Physician** <note>original: *Catholici Physici*; referring here to the true natural philosopher or perhaps the Stone itself as a universal healer</note>. And although several well-meaning **philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophi*</note> have written clearly and distinctly enough about it, **and have illustrated it so exquisitely and sharply that it must be established: he who does not gain wisdom from their writings shall remain a fool forever** <note>original: *atq; ita exquisitè & acutè illustraverunt, ut qui ex scriptis illorum non sapiat, insipiens perpetuò maneat, statuendum sit*</note>: yet it is so ordained and created with the one as with the other **Stone** <note>original: *Lapide*</note> that their **secrets** <note>original: *Geheimnüssen*</note> are revealed only to those who love God. But for those who are not children of light, but only **worldly birds** <note>original: *Weltvögel*</note> and **night owls** <note>original: *Nachteulen*</note>, even those things manifest and clear as the midday sun are nothing but dark riddles, parables, and metaphors.

> **For the spurious eagle does not gaze upon the solar rays, and many are utterly given over to a reprobate mind, so that seeing they may not see, and hearing they may not hear.** <note>original: *Aquila enim spuria radios solares non aspicit, multiq; prorsus dati sunt in reprobum sensum, ut videntes non videant, & audientes non audiant.* This draws on a medieval belief that only a true eagle could look directly at the sun; the "reprobate mind" [reprobum sensum] refers to those whose judgment is fundamentally flawed.</note>

This eye grows dim <note>original: *oculus hic caligar* [caligat]</note>; the unwise man does not recognize these things, and the fool does not understand them. Even if you should crush him in a mortar like crushed grain <note>original: *ptisanas*</note>, with a pestle striking from above, his foolishness will not be taken from him <note>A paraphrase of Proverbs 27:22, emphasizing that some are willfully ignorant of divine truths.</note>.

Therefore, whoever intends to recognize and obtain without error both the **Treasure** or the **Stone** <note>original: *Schatz oder Stein*</note>—be it the eternal or the temporal **highest Good** <note>original: *höchste Gut*</note>—let him fear God and ask for them from Him alone, as the true Giver, according to the saying of the Apostle in James 1: "Every perfect gift descends from the Father of lights" <note>original: *Omne donum perfectum descendit à Patre luminum*</note>. Likewise: "If any of you lacks wisdom, let him ask of God."

The secrets of both these high treasures are presented and explained to us in the Word of the Lord, our Almighty God: the former in the **written word** <note>original: *verbo scripto*; meaning the Holy Scriptures</note>, the latter in the **enacted word** <note>original: *verbo facto*; referring to the Word as expressed through the physical acts of Creation</note>. In the former, the super-celestial **Savior** <note>original: *Salvator*</note>, namely the **Word made flesh** <note>original: *Verbum incarnatum*</note>, is presented to us and to all true **theosophical Magi** <note>original: *Theosophischen Magis*; wise seekers who study the intersection of God and nature</note> to be sought and recognized, as if wrapped in his swaddling clothes. This Word is nothing other than pure fire, spirit, and life—indeed, it is that very spirit which, at the beginning of this world's creation, was **universally** <note>original: *universaliter*</note> poured out by the Holy Trinity and hovered over the **primordial waters** <note>original: *primordialischen Wassern*</note>. Of this **incorporated Word** <note>original: *incorporato*</note> it is said:

—which is granted and given to him alone for this purpose out of grace; and although this **Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note>, as mentioned, has been **incorporated** <note>original: *incorporirt*</note> into all creatures through the Word and blessing of the Lord, it nevertheless lies buried and hidden not only in a more volatile or **fixed** <note>original: *fixer*; an alchemical term for substances that resist fire</note> state, higher or deeper, but is also—according to the form of that **body** <note>original: *corporis*</note> and its dwelling—more noble or base, and easier or harder to separate and extract from one creature than from another.

&emsp;&emsp;But how our bodily life is strengthened and sustained in its **vigor** <note>original: *vigore*; vitality or strength</note> by this **incorporated spirit** <note>original: *Spiritum incorporatum*</note> or corporeal life cannot be more clearly shown and illustrated than through the comparison and example of the **Super-celestial Stone** <note>original: *Lapidis super-coelestis*; a reference to Christ as the spiritual version of the Philosophers' Stone</note> with our human nature. For just as we humans cannot recover in body and soul for eternal salvation and life unless we are first, here in time, **fermented** <note>original: *fermentirt*; in alchemy, fermentation refers to a process that transforms and "multiplies" the spirit of a substance</note> with a mediating nature—which is the **divinized** <note>original: *vergottete*</note> flesh and blood of **Christ** <note>original: *Christi*</note>—by the power of His Spirit and our faith.

->(11.)<-

In the very same way, our **Microcosmic** <note>original: *Microcosmisch*; the "Little World," a term used by early scientists and mystics to describe the human being as a reflection of the entire universe</note> physical life cannot be prolonged or sustained, unless it happens through such a mediating nature, which is a **corporeal spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus corporeus*</note> or a **spiritual body** <note>original: *Corpus spirituale*</note>. Arising from the union of the heavenly, life-giving spirit of the Great World—the Macrocosm—with a pure and delicate **body** <note>original: *corpore*</note> that is part of a tangible and earthly substance, it must not be the incomprehensible **Spirit of the World** <note>original: *Spiritus mundi*</note>, **considered in itself and absolutely** <note>original: *per se & absolutè consideratus*</note>; rather, it must be such a spirit that has taken on a body—that is, one which has been incorporated into God’s creatures.

For it has pleased God that the incomprehensible Light, Spirit, and Life of this temporal and fleeting world should become a tangible body—that is, take on a body and, out of physical things, or rather, pour itself into them and unite with them, so that through this our own physical life might be **preserved** <note>original: *salviret*; from the Latin *salvare*, meaning to save, heal, or keep safe</note>. Therefore, the more noble the **subject of incorporation** <note>original: *Subjectum incorporationis*; the physical matter that serves as the host for the spirit</note> is, the more excellent and powerful is that same [spirit] which is in-

incorporated spirit may be extracted from its **Body** <note>original: *Corpore*</note> (yet not without a body) through Art <note>The "Art" refers to alchemy or spagyric chemistry—the process of separating and purifying substances.</note>; this spirit then has the power, once taken into us, to maintain and sustain this our temporal and transitory life according to every wish and necessity, for as long as God has foreseen and ordained for us to live in this **vale of tears** <note>original: *Jammerthal*; a traditional poetic term for the earthly world of suffering</note>, until the appointed goal of death.

Whichever **Physician** <note>original: *Physicus*; at this time, a physician was specifically a "natural philosopher" who understood the physical world</note> now lays and builds his **principles of Medicine** <note>original: *principia Medicinæ*</note> upon this foundation—which comes from true **Theology** <note>original: *Theologiâ*</note> and fundamentally agrees with the same—he will not easily wander **from the true goal of preserving and saving our fleeting life** <note>original: *à vero scopo servandæ & salvandæ nostræ caducæ vitæ*</note>. For here lies the stone upon which the pagan **followers of Aesculapius** <note>original: *Æsculapii*; referring to traditional doctors who followed ancient Greek medical authorities like Galen</note> and their **rivals** <note>original: *æmuli*</note> have hitherto violently stumbled. This is indeed not to be wondered at, because they did not understand the theological "Emanuel," or the **Word made flesh** <note>original: *verbum incarnatum*</note>, or at least did not sufficiently consider and weigh it. What then could they have fundamentally known or understood of the **business of temporal health** <note>original: *negotio salutis temporalis*; the Latin *salutis* can mean both "health" and "salvation"</note> or of an **earthly Savior** <note>original: *irrdischem Salvatore*; here referring to a universal medicine or panacea that reflects Christ's healing power on a physical level</note>?

->⁕⁕) 13. (⁕⁕<-

Just as, however, no human being may partake of the **Lord**
**Christ** (as the supernatural / divine /
fiery **Tincture-Stone** <note>original: *TincturSteins*; in alchemy, a tincture is a potent substance capable of transforming and perfecting base matter. Here, the author uses it to describe Christ's power to transform the human soul.</note>) either here
in time, or there in eternity, unless he has first
been anointed with the Spirit of **Christ** / in spiritual rebirth /
through the water-bath in the Word / namely
holy Baptism / and has been fermented <note>original: *fermentirt*; used here as a spiritual metaphor for the transformation of the soul, much like yeast changes dough or a catalyst initiates an alchemical reaction.</note> into a child
of **GOD** / which

->⁕⁕) 14. (⁕⁕<-

Just so, every human being—placed into this temporal life by the highest **GOD**—has a **little spark** <note>original: *Füncklein*; often refers to the *scintilla animae* or "spark of the soul," a divine fragment within the human person that connects the individual to the Creator.</note> within them from the light-filled spirit and life of the world. (25.)

...everything hangs and is bound together /
of which **Rhazes** <note>original: *Rasis*; Abu Bakr al-Razi (c. 865–925), a Persian physician and alchemist whose works were fundamental to medieval and Renaissance science</note> says in the *Book of Divinity*: ***"Know that the things of nature are bound together by a subtle artifice, such that in any thing, every other thing exists potentially, even if it is not seen in its actual form."*** <note>original: *Sci-as, res naturæ subtili artificio simul colligatas esse, quod in qualibet re est res quælibet potentiâ, quamvis actu non videatur.*</note> This kinship is to be understood from the *Tree of Philosophy* in this way / namely that Nature or the **Universal World-Spirit** <term>Spiritus mundi universalis; a vital, cosmic energy or "breath" believed by alchemists to permeate all matter and act as the medium of creation</term>, as the first created being after God / first gives and flows life and power into the elements / afterwards, from the mixing of the elements, the seed of all growing things arises / then from the seed grow the **Minerals**, as those which are most closely assigned to the **center of the earth** / however, from the volatile vapors and **exhalations** <note>original: *exhalationibus*; the "fumes" or gases believed to condense within the earth to form metals and minerals</note> of the minerals arise the **Plants** <note>original: *vegetabilia*</note>. The plants feed the **Animals** and the animals along with the best essence of the plants feed / clothe, and provide medicine for Man. Thus all of created nature comes together in Man / as in a **Center** / and each partakes of the other / so that from this it is not unjustly concluded / that ***the matter of the Philosopher’s Stone is found everywhere.*** <note>original: *lapidis Philos. materiam ubiq; reperiri.*</note>

<meta>This page concludes the general discussion from page 25 regarding the interconnectedness of all things via the World-Spirit. (26.)

From all of these matters which have been discussed generally so far, these four following conclusions are well worth noting. Namely, because the **World-Spirit** <term>Spiritus mundi; a vital, cosmic energy believed by alchemists to permeate all matter</term>, or **Universal Nature**, of which we speak here, is viewed by the philosophers from a distance, but is grasped and obtained in close proximity. 

In its most distant sense <note>original: *Remotissimè*</note>, this spirit is incomprehensibly elevated and remote, far above all the **Elements** and the **heavenly spheres**. In a nearer sense, it dwells and rests partly within the elements (where it is still universal, simple, and more or less <note>original: *magis minusque*</note> spiritual). It also dwells partly in "elemental things" <note>original: *elementatis*; physical matter composed of the four elements</note>, where it exists in a particular and specific way <note>original: *particulariter & specificè*</note>, tangibly whole and in bodily form. 

Thus, in its closest and most specific form <note>original: *propinquè & particulariter*</note>, it is found most fixedly and powerfully in those "growths" created at the **center of the elements**, which are the **Minerals** alone.

For this reason, it pleased me to refer to and include what a learned Frenchman named **Guillaume Traignier** <note>original: *Gulielmus Trognianus*; likely Guillaume Traignier, a 16th-century author associated with alchemical aphorisms</note> has written concisely <note>original: *Laconicè*; in the brief, pithy style of the ancient Spartans</note> regarding this:

->thus:<-

<summary>The author concludes that while the World-Spirit is a vast, cosmic force, it is most tangibly and powerfully concentrated within minerals at the earth's center. This justifies the alchemical focus on the mineral kingdom as the most potent source of the universal essence.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the philosophical argument regarding the "Universal Nature" or World-Spirit. It elaborates on the Hermetic principle of "As above, so below," specifically focusing on how the celestial (Heaven) acts as both the outer container of the universe and the secret, vital spirit within every individual object.</meta>

(27.)

Everything that exists proceeds from the **heavens** <term>Coelum; in this context, it refers not just to the sky, but to the celestial spheres and the divine source of cosmic influence</term> and shares in the nature of the heavens. For nothing is as natural as for that which is born to share the nature and substance of its parent. Since the heaven is the universal parent of all lower things, it is necessary that all these lower things share in the nature and substance of heaven. 

And so, heaven is hidden within all things below, while on the outside it is manifest and visible beyond all things. Just as it is spread most widely in its body upon the surface of the world, it is likewise most tightly contracted in its spirit at the center of the world. 

Therefore, the heaven is the highest of all things in its body, set on high; and yet again, the heaven is the deepest of all things, hidden in the spiritual depths <note>original: *in imo spirituoso*; referring to the "inner heaven" or the vital, energetic core of matter</note>. And just as it encloses and covers all things with its body, so it permeates and fills all things with its spirit. For heaven is visibly present everywhere in its own form, and yet heaven is also within all things by its **virtue** <term>Virtus; the inherent power or specific medicinal and alchemical property of a substance</term>. And just as nothing is more manifest than the heaven throughout all things, so again, nothing is more hidden within all things than the heaven.

This heaven, or the **Philosophers’ Stone** <term>Lapis Philosophorum; the legendary alchemical substance sought to perfect matter and achieve spiritual enlightenment</term>, is in all things, and it passes through every thing in the whole world.

And all things are full of its virtue <note>original: *virtute*; referring to the inherent spiritual power or medicinal efficacy of a substance</note>, and nothing is void of its essence and power (except for that which is dead and is no longer counted among living creatures).

**fire against Nature** <note>original: *ignem contra Naturam*; in alchemy, this refers to corrosive mineral acids like vitriol or aqua fortis, rather than literal flame</note>. Because of this, **Paracelsus’s Elixir of Gold for long life** <note>original: *Paracelsi Elixir auri ad longam vitam*; Paracelsus (1493–1541) was a revolutionary Swiss physician who championed the use of chemicals in medicine</note>, which he teaches one to prepare **through very powerful corrosives**, is also held to be very suspicious and dangerous.

For **a transition from one extreme to another is not possible, except through a skillful and suitable medium** <note>original: *De uno enim extremo ad aliud non datur transitus, nisi per medium habile & conveniens*; a philosophical principle stating that two radically different substances, like a hard stone and a soft human body, require an intermediary to interact</note>.

Such a medium between minerals and our human nature are vegetable and animal things, as they are already related and more pleasing to our nature; therefore, they can make the mentioned minerals (when united and bound with them **according to the laws of the art** <note>original: *secundum artis leges*; the "art" refers to alchemy/chemistry</note>) much more lovely and agreeable. **Otherwise, nature will not easily suffer or endure a leap of this kind** <note>original: *Alias natura ejusmodi saltus non facile patietur ac perferet*; a variation of the famous phrase "nature does not make leaps"</note>. Although this is accomplished most excellently and perfectly with the nature of gold (for gold is the **limit of absolute and completed metallic perfection** <note>original: *terminus absolutæ & consummatæ metallicæ perfectionis*</note>), it may nonetheless also be carried out with other minerals in which gold still lies **imperfect**, or as if in **potential**. <meta>This page continues the alchemical argument that medicinal substances derived from plants and animals (the "volatile") must be stabilized using mineral principles to be truly effective. ->❀ ( 35. ) ❀<-

*Human life is short, and very much like a fragile bubble,* <note>original: "Vita hominum brevis est, fragiliq; simillima bullæ"</note>
*Which must be protected by us through the medical art.*
*But you ask, by what means will it be supported and made strong;*
*And even what herbs are there, from which we should seek aid?*
*Turn both your eyes and your mind away from the combustible,* <note>original: "A combustibili"; referring to organic materials that burn away or decay easily</note>
*And that which is more fixed, take this, prefer it, and love it.* <note>In alchemy, "fixed" substances are those that remain stable and do not evaporate or change when exposed to intense heat.</note>
*For it is certain that grasses, supported by too weak a step,*
*Are unable to extend a short life.*
*More surely, by digging minerals from the womb of the earth,*
*You will hasten to find helping aid.*
*Here is where the celestial powers concentrate the innermost*
*Balsams* <note>original: "Balsama"; here used to mean the life-preserving essential fluids or "oils" of nature</note> *at the very roots, and where they flow into the metals.*
*Yet if the grace of herbs has taken hold of you, or if*
*The gifts which the Microcosm* <note>original: "Microcosmus"; the human being viewed as a miniature version of the entire universe</note> *possesses delight you;*
*From this or from those, take only what is fixed, leaving behind*
*Those things which, like fugitives, do not endure the fire.*
*For they are not sufficient for the root of life,*
*And those things which gave quickly, also pass away quickly.*
*But those things which are long-lasting prolong life,*
*Having attained the fixed degrees of fire.*
*All things must be subjected to Master Vulcan,* <note>original: "Vulcano"; the Roman god of fire, representing the alchemist's furnace and the refining power of heat</note>
*Who makes things clearer through purification;*
*He separates and restores; he who casts down to the shadows*
*Of cold death, also restores to new life.*
*So that they may take on the nature of the fifth essence* <note>original: "Ætheris... quintam"; the Quintessence or "Aether," the pure, incorruptible element of the heavens</note> *by imitation,*
*And the pure elements may unite with a fixed foot.*
*What if all things were even subject to a single water,* <note>original: "lymphæ"; referring to a primal alchemical solvent or the "mercurial" principle of life</note>
*Which flows into all things with life of every kind?*
*From here a new light arises, and reborn virtue gleams.*
*From here you will be able to provide for a short life.*

But most of all, this **fixed spirit** <note>original: "figirte Geist"; a "fixed" spirit is a substance that was once volatile but has been processed to remain stable under heat</note> is obtained and received from us...

<summary>A didactic poem arguing that mineral-based medicines are superior to herbal ones because they possess "fixity" and can survive the refining fire of the alchemist. It concludes that only substances that endure the fire (the "Master Vulcan") can truly prolong human life.</summary>

Since, however, the earth is viewed **in a twofold way** <note>original Latin: *duplici modo*</note> by the **philosophers** <term>Philosophers: in alchemical literature, "the Philosophers" refers to the sages and masters of alchemy throughout history, such as Hermes Trismegistus or Geber.</term>: **either in the center or on the surface** <note>original Latin: *vel in centro, vel in superficie*</note>. The latter is before everyone’s eyes, but the former is known only to the **philosophers** and is extracted from the **inner and deeper viscera** <note>original Latin: *interioribus & profundioribus visceribus*</note> of the superficial earth. Therefore, I see fit to mention and indicate something here regarding both types of earth.

Concerning the central earth, there are as many different **judgments and opinions** <note>original Latin: *Judicia und Opiniones*</note> as there are findings regarding **living gold** <note>original Latin: *auro vivo*</note>. So that I may set aside most of these, several well-grounded **philosophers** understand this to be the **central salt** <note>original Latin: *Sal centrale*</note>, which is leached **out of this superficial and elemental earth** <note>original Latin: *ex superficiali & elementali istâ terrâ*</note>. **Likewise:** When the **earthly elements** <note>original Latin: *elementa terrea*</note> have become earth once again and are then also leached. (Whether the **reduction** has occurred through violent **calcination** <term>Calcination: the process of heating a substance to high temperatures in the presence of air to oxidize it or reduce it to a friable powder; a key step in purification.</term>...

Stripped of the variety of its colors, it rejoices with its companion, dyed with a color like snow. It builds on the previous page's focus on universal nature and the "artist's" choice of methods.</meta>

<page-num>53</page-num>
<sig>e</sig>
<meta>catchword: sen</meta>

It is, however, necessary to know here that the **Philosophers** <note>In this context, "Philosophers" refers to alchemists, the "natural philosophers" of the early modern period.</note> speak of two kinds of **Mercuries**, each of which may perform a special and distinct philosophical work. One is called the **simple**, the other the **double**. Of the double—which namely consists of earthly <note>original: "iridischen," likely a variant of "irdischen" (terrestrial/earthly)</note> and heavenly principles—these **Tables** and the Polish author **Sendivogius** <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636) was a famous Polish alchemist and physician known for his theories on the "food of life" in the air (oxygen).</note> treat.

And [so do] my **Additions to chapter 7** <note>original: "Additiones ad cap. 7."</note>, in which place I also dealt with the **simple Mercury** in the **dry way** <note>original: "in viâ siccâ"; a method involving high heat and solid materials rather than liquid solutions.</note>, namely the **sweet crystals of common salt** <note>original: "Cristallis dulcibus salis communis"</note>, as those which are most closely related to elemental simplicity or universal nature. 

But of that in the **wet way** <note>original: "in viâ humidâ"; a method involving liquid solvents and dissolution.</note> I will make little mention here; it consists solely of earthly principles (leaving the heavenly irradiation <note>The perceived celestial or "astral" influence on matter.</note> in its own dignity, but not seeking it at this time). 

Its **practice** <note>The laboratory procedure or practical application.</note> is briefly as follows: That ten times as much by weight of the **spiritual water** <note>original: "aquæ spirituosæ"</note>, however **rectified** <note>original: "utcunque rectificatæ"; purified through repeated distillation.</note>, is taken to one part of both well-purified and refined **salts**, the **fixed and the volatile** <note>original: "Salien / fixi & vo- latilis"; referring to salts that resist heat and those that vaporize.</note>, and [added] to a glass, of...

...[vessel] whose **containing capacity** <note>original: *capacitas continens*</note> is 5 or 6 times larger than the space the **contents** <note>original: *contenti*</note> occupy, [so that it] may be **putrefied** <note>original: *putrificirt*; in alchemy, putrefaction is a process of "digesting" or rotting matter in a sealed vessel to break it down to its primal state.</note> until the **matter** <note>original: *Materia*</note> in the glass has become entirely black. Then distill it with great diligence; before the water, an oil comes over like a salt. Guard this well, for it is very volatile. Distill further, and a **phlegmatic water** <note>original: *Phlegmatisch Wasser*; a watery, low-potency byproduct of distillation.</note> comes over; cast that away. At the bottom remains a black pitch; distill this strongly, and an oil will also come out, similar to the aforementioned oil. You should purify the lower oil; then join both oils—the **upper and the lower** <note>original: *superius & inferius*</note>—together like male and female seed. Thus you have the **simple Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Mercurium Philosophorum*; not common quicksilver, but a purified alchemical agent.</note>, which is prepared having been taken only from the **lower sphere or globe of the world** <note>original: *sphæra vel globo mundi*; referring to the terrestrial or elemental realm as opposed to the celestial.</note>. This you may either set to **coagulate** <note>original: *coaguliren*; to solidify or fix a substance.</note> alone and **by itself** <note>original: *per se*</note>, or bring to **perfection** <note>original: *Perfection*</note> with a twelfth part of gold. Thus the **essence of gold** <note>original: *Goldwesen*</note> is killed within it, and then born anew once more.

<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 58 regarding the composition of minerals. The previous page established that stones are essentially "earthly water" or hardened slime; this page explains the mechanism by which they grow and why they are categorized as "stony earth."</meta>

(59.)

The **matter** <note>original: *Materia*</note> from which these stones grow is that very same **subtle earthly substance** <note>original: *subtile terreum*</note> which separates itself from water and, in the belly of the earth, attaches itself to a suitable soil wherever it may encounter it. Within and with this soil, it **incorporates itself** <note>original: *incorporirt*</note>, no differently than how—**outside the earth** <note>original: *extra terram*</note>—in flowing waters, **slime** <term>slime<gloss>Mucilago; in historical natural philosophy, this refers to a viscous, sediment-like "earthiness" or organic residue found in liquids</gloss></term> or earthiness clings to stones. Or, to speak more thoroughly: sand and stones in the water, and the soils within the earth, pull toward themselves whatever is earthy in the water through a **magnetic force** <note>original: *vi Magneticâ*</note>; from this, they grow and increase. Thus, the earthly nature always **predominates** <note>original: *prædominirt*</note> here—as indicated—to a **greater or lesser degree** <note>original: *secundum magis minusque*</note>. Therefore, it is not unreasonable that they are also **named** <note>original: *denominirt*</note> after the earth and are called **stony earth** <note>original: *terra lapidea*</note>.

Furthermore, another distinction is to be noted: those stones which are translucent have a watery nature, yet—as mentioned—are **produced** <note>original: *progenerirt*</note> from the **water’s subtle earth**. However, those which **exhibit opacity** <note>original: *opacitatem præ se feriren*</note> are of an earthly nature and come **materially** <note>original: *materialiter*</note>...

<summary>This page explains that stones grow by pulling earthy particles from water through a magnetic-like force and incorporating them into the soil of the earth's "belly." It also distinguishes between translucent stones (watery in nature) and opaque stones (earthy in nature).</summary>

--- Page 156 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of mineral formation, specifically focusing on the selection and distillation of "mineral earth"—identified here as bismuth ore—and its sympathetic relationship with the lunar cycle. It follows the previous page's description of how different earths and stones are coagulated by internal heat.</meta>

The location is determined; in one place it is found to be nobler and better than in another. Among these, that which already leans toward a metallic nature has the prize and preference, being a **mineral earth in its very essence** <note>original: *terra mineralis ipso actu*</note>. For this reason, the **ore of magnesia** <note>original: *minera magnesiæ*</note>, that is, **bismuth ore** <term>bismuth ore<gloss>Wißmut Erz</gloss></term>, is considered by some to be the best. 

However, the test will reveal it. One should take 9 pounds of it, before it has been touched by any fire, put it into a retort, and place a large **receiver** <term>receiver<gloss>recipienten</gloss></term> before it, and distill it **according to the degrees of fire** <note>original: *secundum gradus ignis*; referring to the traditional alchemical practice of gradually increasing heat</note> for one hour or twelve. Thus one obtains a white water, entirely sweet. 

Rectify <note>To "rectify" in alchemy is to purify a liquid through repeated distillation to separate the pure spirit from its impurities</note> this for a third time, and it will become even purer and sweeter. Put 1 pound of this into a glass, well-sealed with wax and a bladder, but ensure the glass is one-third empty. Place it in a location where it may stand undisturbed, and you will see that the water increases during the **full moon** <note>original: *plenilunio*</note> and the glass becomes completely full; and when the moon wanes, the water will also decrease. During the **new moon** <note>original: *novilunio*</note>, it will lose another part in addition to the part it had grown, and it will become quite heavy; yet [it] will

(64.)

...taken as **particular works** <note>original: *particularischen operibus*; specific alchemical operations rather than the "Universal Work"</note> and used in two senses; namely, the first and second birth.

By the **first birth** is understood how the **flint** <gloss>Kißling</gloss> or **quartz** <gloss>Quartz</gloss> is given into the hand of the artist by nature itself, formed in the mountains or waters, solid and beautifully transparent. In this case, the selected **clear pebbles** <note>original: *calculi lucidi*</note> from flowing waters are considered the best (for in sand or grit, however pretty it may be, the inner fire is not yet in such **vigor** <gloss>vigore</gloss> or strength as in the aforementioned pebbles). And although the **mucilage** <term>mucilage<gloss>Mucilago</gloss>; a viscous, slime-like substance believed by alchemists to be the precursor to mineral growth</term> or slime, which hangs on the stones in flowing waters, is both the **prime matter** <note>original: *prima materia*; the fundamental substance from which all things are created</note> and the **growth** <gloss>incrementum</gloss> of the stones (since nothing can be forced from raw **flint** <gloss>silice</gloss> by the violence of fire), when made into a water, either with or without the **spirit of common salt** <note>original: *Spiritu salis communis*; an early term for hydrochloric acid</note> or a similar **vehicle** <note>a substance used to carry or dissolve an active ingredient</note>, it is a true and natural **solvent** <gloss>dissolvens</gloss> for all stones and metals, about which **Beguinus** <note>Jean Beguin (c. 1550–1620), a famous Flemish chemist and author of "Tyrocinium Chymicum," one of the first chemistry textbooks</note> writes: "From all metals a fragrant spirit can be drawn out, by means of a viscous mineral [water]..." <note>original Latin: *Ex omnibus metallis spiritus fragrans elici potest, mediante aqua viscosa minerali*</note>
