The **Multiplication** <term>multiplicatio: the process of increasing the quantity or the potency of the alchemical medicine</term> of the aforementioned white stone is done in quantity with its own first matter. However, if you wish to make the **Red Elixir**, take half of the white stone and keep it over a dry and tempered fire for four natural days <note>A "natural day" is a full 24-hour cycle</note>. At the end, you will have the red medicine, which is called the **complete Red Elixir**, in which nothing remains to be done except the **projection** <term>projectio: the final act of the Great Work, where the Elixir is cast upon molten base metal to transform it into gold</term>. This Elixir differs from the Stone. For the Stone is a spirit extracted from our incorruptible body, holding **quicksilver** <note>original: "argentum vivum"</note>—that is, the flowing water—within the body—that is, the earth—with a firm containment and nature. **Elixir**, however, in Greek sounds like **ferment** <term>fermentum: a substance that, like yeast in dough, has the power to transform and "leaven" a much larger mass of material into its own nature</term>, because just as a little bit of fer-

(134.)

...forth: I nevertheless hold that common silver may also take its place here with good benefit and renown.

Regarding the Mercury of the Sun <note>original: *Mercurium Solis*; a liquid or "running" form of gold, considered a key alchemical solvent</note>, however, which appears red in color like blood and yet flows like other quicksilver <note>mercury</note>, it should be known that there are many ways to bring gold into a dry, flowing water. Among these, more than eight are known to me, each of which follows and maintains its own particular method of preparation <note>original: *præparation*</note>, and thus demonstrates particular effects <note>original: *effectus*</note>. None of these methods are alike, and almost all are white in their outward appearance <note>original: *in suo manifesto*; referring to the visible, external state of the substance</note>.

But the very best and most skillful method is that which reveals itself publicly in its divine, natural, shining color. It is also of a more fixed <term>fixed|A term describing a substance that is stable in fire and does not evaporate or fly away when heated</term> condition than many others. ### (142.)

...congenial and familiar, which alone it touches and strikes while leaving all others unharmed, and clearly demonstrates its active force by leaving behind an evident mark. So it is that, having left behind those superficial and flimsy <note>original: *refollaceis*; likely referring to something outer or leaf-like in structure</note> external rays—since they provide only an idle and sterile light without the energy of vital virtues—we are concerned in this work only with those rays which, together with the light, administer the effective and living economy of the sun's essential powers; and which most generously pour into and flow into the body of **gold** <note>original: *auri*; often symbolized by the sun in alchemy</note>, once it is well-opened and sufficiently prepared, as a subject that is harmonious and appropriate to itself.

Castus:

### (143.) 50
<meta>library/shelf marks: 608; sig: P3</meta>

To say something regarding the *manner of proceeding* <note>original: *De modo autem procedendi*</note>, one requires two things for the completion of this work. First, the **Olympic Solar Fire** <note>A term for sunlight concentrated through a lens or mirror, used as a "pure" heat source in alchemy</note>, called by some *natural fire* <note>original: *ignis naturalis*</note>, but named by *Paracelsus* <note>The famous Swiss-German physician and alchemist (1493–1541) who championed the use of chemicals and minerals in medicine</note> the **invisible fire** (and he says further) by which we mean the *rays* <note>original: *radios*</note> of the sun. This fire reveals itself through a steel mirror or crystals, making it visible to the eyes and perceptible to the other senses; it also demonstrates its power and *effect* <note>original: *effect*</note> to such an extent that one can use it to melt metals and other liquid, fatty things freely upon a table. The ancients wrote nothing at all about this.

Secondly, one requires for this a **vessel**, in or upon which this work of *perfection* <note>original: *perfection*; the final stage of the alchemical process</note> is performed. In this text, it appears as though it must be made of glass and be sealed, because he says: *“Guard the smoke, and take care that none of it escapes. Likewise, when it has been cooled and opened, etc.”* <note>original: *Custodi fumum, & cave, ne fugiat aliquid de illo. Item quando fuerit infrigidatum & apertum, &c.*</note> From this I also suspect that although the other

# LETTER

## Of John Pontanus, the Great Philosopher,
### in which the Stone, which they call the
### Philosopher's [Stone], is discussed.

I, John Pontanus, have traveled through many regions so that I might recognize something certain regarding the <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>original: *lapide Philosophorum*; the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting long life</note>, and as if circling the whole world, I found false deceivers and not philosophers. However, by always studying and doubting in many ways, I found the truth. But even when I recognized the <term>matter</term> <note>The raw material or "First Matter" used to begin the alchemical work</note>, I erred two hundred times before I found the true matter, operation, and practice. First, I began and discovered the operations of the matter through <term>putrefactions</term> <note>original: *putrefactiones*; a stage in alchemy where the substance is broken down, often turning black</note> of nine months; I also placed it in the <term>bath of Mary</term> <note>original: *balneo Mariae*; a water bath or double boiler used for gentle heating</note> for some time, and similarly I erred. Truly, I placed it in the fire of <term>calcination</term> <note>original: *calcinationis*; the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder</note> for three months, and I worked poorly <note>Meaning the experiment was unsuccessful</note>.

->All<-

<summary>This page introduces the "Letter of John Pontanus," a classic alchemical text. Pontanus recounts his extensive travels and his many failed experimental attempts—including long periods of putrefaction and heating—before finally discovering the secret of the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>

<keywords>John Pontanus, Philosopher's Stone, alchemical practice, putrefaction, bain-marie, calcination, history of science, alchemical errors</keywords>

--- Page 254 ---
<meta>Continuing the letter of John Pontanus from page 151. On the previous page, Pontanus described his initial failures and errors. Here, he shifts his focus to his rejection of common alchemical methods in favor of the secret "fire" revealed in the works of Hermes Trismegistus.</meta>

(152)

all types of <term>distillations</term> <note>original: *destillationum*; the process of purifying a liquid by heating and cooling</note> and <term>sublimations</term> <note>original: *sublimationum*; heating a solid until it becomes a vapor and then solidifies again, used to purify substances</note>, just as the Philosophers say, or seem to say—such as <term>Geber</term> <note>Jabir ibn Hayyan, a foundational figure in 8th-century alchemy</note>, Archelaus, and almost all others—I have handled, and I found nothing. Finally, I attempted to perfect the subject of the entire art of <term>Alchemy</term> in every way that can be devised, including those performed by way of <term>dung</term> <note>original: *fimum*; horse dung was frequently used as a source of steady, low-temperature heat</note>, the <term>bath</term> <note>original: *balneum*; likely the *balneum Mariae* or water bath</note>, ashes, and other fires of many kinds which are found in the books of the Philosophers, but I found nothing of value.

For this reason, I studied the books of the Philosophers for three continuous years; especially <term>Hermes</term> <note>referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the foundational "Emerald Tablet" and other alchemical works are attributed</note> alone, whose brief words encompass the whole <term>Stone</term> <note>original: *lapidem*; the Philosopher's Stone</note>; although he speaks obscurely of the superior and the inferior, of heaven and of earth. Therefore, our instrument by which the <term>matter</term> is brought into being in the first, second, and third work is not the fire of the bath, nor of dung, nor of ashes, nor of the other fires that the Philosophers have placed in their books. What, therefore, is that fire which completes the entire work from the beginning to the end? Certainly, the Philosophers have hidden it. But I, moved by piety, wish to declare it to you, along with the completion of the whole work.

<summary>John Pontanus recounts his rejection of common alchemical heating methods after years of failure, turning instead to the cryptic writings of Hermes Trismegistus to discover the "secret fire" necessary for the Great Work.</summary>
<keywords>John Pontanus, Geber, Hermes Trismegistus, alchemical fire, distillation, sublimation, Philosopher's Stone, secret fire</keywords>

(153)

Therefore, the <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>original: *Lapis Philosophorum*; the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting eternal life</note> is one, but it is called by many names, and until you recognize it, it will be difficult for you. For it is of the air, of fire, of earth, and it is <term>phlegmatic, choleric, sanguine, and melancholic</term> <note>referring to the four humors of ancient and medieval medicine, suggesting the Stone encompasses all physical temperaments</note>; it is sulfurous, and it is likewise <term>quicksilver</term> <note>original: *argentum vivum*; mercury</note>, and it has many <term>superfluities</term> <note>original: *superfluitates*; impurities or excess matter</note>, which through the living GOD are converted into the true <term>essence</term> by means of our fire.

And he who separates anything from the <term>subject</term> <note>original: *subjecto*; the starting material or base matter of the alchemical work</note>, thinking it necessary to do so, truly knows nothing of Philosophy; for the superfluous, the unclean, the base, the dregs, and finally the entire substance of the subject, is perfected into a fixed spiritual body by means of our fire. And the wise have never withheld this: yet few reach the art, because they think such superfluous and unclean matter must be separated. 

Now it is necessary to draw out the properties of our fire, and whether it suits our <term>matter</term> according to the manner I have described—namely, that it may be <term>transmuted</term>—since that fire does not burn the matter, nor separate anything from the matter, nor segregate the pure parts from the impure (as all Philosophers say), but converts the whole subject into purity; it does not sub- <note>The text cuts off here at the prefix "sub-", which is completed by the catchword "limas" to form *sublimas*, meaning "you sublime" or "you elevate."</note>

<summary>Pontanus explains that the Philosopher's Stone is a single, multifaceted substance that contains all elements and humors. He reveals the crucial alchemical secret that nothing should be discarded from the base matter, as the "secret fire" purifies and transforms even the "unclean" parts into the final Stone.</summary>

<header>❧(154)☙</header>

it refines just as <term>Geber</term> <note>original: *Geber*; likely referring to Jabir ibn Hayyan, the 8th-century "father of chemistry" whose works were central to medieval alchemy</note> performs his <term>sublimations</term> <note>the process of heating a substance until it becomes a vapor and then becomes solid again upon cooling</note> (and likewise <term>Arnoldus</term> <note>original: *Arnoldus*; Arnold of Villanova, a famous 13th-century physician and alchemist</note> and others speaking of sublimations and <term>distillations</term>), and it completes the work in a short time. It is mineral, it is uniform, it is continuous; it does not evaporate unless it is over-excited. It participates in the nature of sulfur, yet it is taken from somewhere other than the <term>matter</term> <note>the base substance being transformed</note> itself. It destroys all things, it dissolves and congeals; likewise, it both congeals and <term>calcinates</term> <note>reduces a substance to a powder through heat</note>. It requires art to be discovered, and it is a shortcut without any expense—or at least very little. That fire is of a moderate heat, for with a gentle fire the entire work is perfected, and it simultaneously performs all the necessary sublimations.

Those who would read Geber and all the other Philosophers, even if they were to live for a hundred thousand years, would not understand this; for it is only through deep and profound meditation that this fire is found. Only then can it be understood in books, and not before. Therefore, the great error of this art is failing to find the fire which converts the whole matter into the true <term>Philosopher’s Stone</term>. 

Study the fire, then; for if I had found it at the start, I would not have erred two hundred times in my practical experiments upon the matter. For this reason, I do not wonder if so many and such great men to the work...

They err, they have erred, and they will err, because the Philosophers have not identified the proper agent—except for one, who is named <term>Artephius</term> <note>original: *Artephius*; a legendary 12th-century alchemical figure often cited as the only one to speak clearly about the "secret fire"</note>. But he speaks for himself; and had I not read Artephius and sensed the meaning of his words, I would never have reached the completion of the work.

The <term>practice</term> <note>the actual laboratory procedure</note> is indeed this: Let the substance be taken and ground as diligently as possible by physical grinding, and then set to the fire. Let the proportion of the fire be known—specifically, let it only "excite" the matter. In a short time, that fire, without any further manual intervention, will surely complete the entire work.

For it will perform <term>putrefaction</term> <note>the stage where the matter breaks down or "dies" to be reborn</note>, corruption, generation, and perfection. It will cause the three principal colors to appear: <term>black</term> <note>Latin: *nigrum*; the stage of decay or *nigredo*</note>, <term>white</term> <note>Latin: *album*; the stage of purification or *albedo*</note>, and <term>red</term> <note>Latin: *rubeum*; the final stage of success or *rubedo*</note>. 

Through the mediation of our fire, the <term>medicine</term> <note>the transformative substance or Philosopher's Stone</note> will be multiplied if it is joined with crude matter—not only in its quantity but also in its power. Therefore, you must know to seek your fire with all your strength, and you will reach your goal; for the fire performs the whole work and is the key to all...

<summary>Pontanus credits the alchemist Artephius for revealing the "secret fire" and explains that once the matter is ground and heated correctly, the fire completes the transformation through the three color stages (black, white, red) automatically.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the letter of John Pontanus regarding the "secret fire" and begins a new section comparing celestial and terrestrial fires. It continues the thought from the previous page: "the key to all the secrets of the Philosophers."</meta>

### ❦ (154.) ❦

...of the Philosophers, which they never revealed. But if you have thought well and deeply upon what was said before regarding the properties of fire, you will know, and not otherwise. Moved by compassion, I have written these things; but to satisfy you: the fire is not transmuted with the matter, because it is not of the matter, as I said above. Therefore, I wished to say these things and to warn the prudent, lest they consume their money uselessly, but that they may know what they ought to seek: in this way they may arrive at the truth of the art, and not otherwise. Farewell.

Accordingly, according to the <term>Hermetic Tablet</term> <note>original: *Hermetischer Taffel*; also known as the Emerald Tablet, the foundational text of alchemy attributed to Hermes Trismegistus</note>, that which is above is equal to that which is below, and <term>the other way around</term> <note>original: *vice versâ*</note>. R 3 (173.)

[I have] refrained from naming the reasons by their public names; it is enough what an ancient philosopher says of it: *The best Mercury is brought to us in skins from Montpellier* <note>original Latin: *Optimus Mercurius apportatur nobis in coriis de monte Pessulano.* Montpellier was a famous center for medicine and alchemy in France.</note>. These are now the three **ingredients** <term>ingredientia</term> from whose **mixture** <term>mixtura</term> the **Fire of Pontanus** <note>Johannes Pontanus was a famous alchemical author whose "Epistola" describes a "secret fire" that is not common fire, but a mineral heat that works on the matter without burning it.</note> consists. This [fire] is indeed **excited** by **coal-fire** <term>Kohlenfewer</term> and is kept in a steady state of burning by it (during which one at times sees pleasant, beautiful colored flames like a rainbow).

However, one must not overdo it with the **exciting fire** <term>ignis excitans</term>, **lest the subject fly away from its agent and be dispersed** <note>original Latin: *ne ipsum patiens ab agente suo avolet ac dispergatur.* In alchemy, the "patient" is the substance being worked on, and the "agent" is the heat or chemical acting upon it. Too much heat causes the substance to evaporate or explode before it can be fixed.</note>. Therefore Pontanus says: **It does not evaporate, unless it is too greatly excited** <note>original: *Non evaporat, nisi nimium excitetur.*</note>. Its proper governance and **degree** <term>gradus</term> is, as he says, **with moderate ignition** <note>original: *cum mediocri ignitione.*</note>. This **mineral fire** <term>mineral-Feuer</term> also gives off a warmth or heat of its own through the **sulfur flames** <term>Sulphurflammen</term>. In and with this [internal heat], it **nourishes** <note>original: *fovirt*; meaning to cherish or keep warm, as a bird broods over an egg.</note> and **perfects** <note>original: *perficirt*</note> the **matter** <term>materiam</term> much more naturally than any other borrowed or foreign **heat** <term>calor</term> could. This occurs in a short time (as Pontanus promises) and with little expense, proceeding **through the degrees and colors** <note>original Latin: *per gradus & colores*; referring to the standard stages of the alchemical work marked by color changes—black, white, and red.</note>.

(175.)

A 4

...in which the invisible fire is meant to strike out from the visible flames and incorporate itself: To state it in a single word, this is a **metallic mass** <term>metallische massa</term>, consisting of two bodies of a cold nature, of which one must be a spiritual, dry, and running water, while the other must be an earthly body, yet purely filed or **calcinated** <term>calciniert</term> <note>Calcination is the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a fine powder or ash.</note>. These are formed together into a paste or **softening mass** <term>malagma</term> <note>A malagma is a soft, pliable medicinal or alchemical preparation, similar to a poultice.</note>, of which **Pontanus** <note>Johannes Pontanus, a 15th-century alchemist famous for his "Epistle on the Secret Fire."</note> says: *Let it be taken and ground with physical grinding, etc.* <note>original Latin: "Sumatur & teratur contritione physica, &c."</note>

Which of the two bodies are specifically suited for this above others, he makes understood with two words from the cited **Hermetic Tablet** <note>original Latin: "tabulâ Hermetis"; referring to the Emerald Tablet, a foundational text of Hermeticism and alchemy.</note>: **the superior** <note>original Latin: "superiori"</note> and **the inferior** <note>original Latin: "inferiori"</note>.

Indeed, this work can also be performed with only one of them, whichever it may be, provided that it has first been reduced by art into its running water, and thus made into an open or spiritual metal (for without a spiritual body, nothing exalted, philosophical, or truly worthy of the art of **tinctures** <term>tincturisch</term> <note>The "tincture" refers to the transformative power of the Philosopher's Stone to change base metals into gold.</note> can be achieved here; therefore, those works which also, but not...

# THE EPISTLE OF HAIMON
### concerning the
## FOUR PHILOSOPHICAL STONES,
### which derive their matter from the lesser world. <note>original Latin: *minore mundo*; in alchemical thought, the "lesser world" or microcosm refers to the human body. This title indicates that the four "stones" or transformative substances are prepared from materials of human origin.</note>

Since it is known to all who are skilled in the art that there are four **Stones** in Philosophy <note>The "Stones" refer to the various stages or substances required to reach the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, about which the **Philosophers** <note>The "Philosophers" here refers to historical alchemists such as Hermes Trismegistus, Geber, or Lully.</note> have written with great obscurity and have hidden the methods of the art through various riddles, each of which is equal to the others in its operation and effect; nevertheless, many faithful laborers have been unable to reach the practical application of these because of a lack of sufficient understanding. It seems worthwhile for those who, divinely enlightened and properly instructed, bring some light to this subject and rescue the preparation of these stones from their obscurity, so that the steps of good workers may be directed onto the right path, and out of this dark de- <note>The text cuts off mid-word "deserto" (desert), which is completed on the following page.</note>

Therefore, because of the great labor I have endured for a long time by working with such operations—though I am conscious of my own insignificance in other matters—I will endeavor to explain more clearly just one method of these, according to the grace poured into me by the Lord God Most High, who alone works wonderfully in this matter, and no one else.

Pay attention, therefore, to the words of Aristotle <note>This refers to the "Pseudo-Aristotle," a name medieval and Renaissance alchemists used to attribute their own writings to the famous Greek philosopher to grant them authority.</note>, who says: Take the <term>animal stone</term> <note>original: *lapidem animalem*; a substance derived from living creatures, traditionally associated with the "soul" of matter.</note>, the <term>vegetable stone</term> <note>original: *vegetabilem*; a substance derived from plants, associated with the "spirit" or the power of growth.</note>, the <term>mineral stone</term> <note>original: *mineralem*; a substance derived from the earth, associated with the physical "body" or salt.</note>, etc. Indeed, there have been many who believed that these words described three different stones or three different things. I, however, say that it is only one thing, which is simultaneously mineral, vegetable, and animal.

I say further: This stone is likened in a certain way to mineral things, and is found at all times in every human being, and it can be changed into any color, and contains within itself all the <term>elements</term> <note>The four classical elements: Earth, Air, Fire, and Water, which alchemists sought to balance.</note>, and is called the <term>lesser world</term> <note>original Latin: *minor Mundus*; the "microcosm." Here I find these names: SCORPIO, DIABESTE, and REBIS. <note><term>Scorpio</term> often represents the corrosive, "stinging" stage of the work; <term>Diabeste</term> is an obscure term likely referring to a specific mineral or a "piercing" substance; <term>Rebis</term> (from the Latin *res bina*) is the "double thing," the union of male and female or spirit and matter.</note>

Leaving all those other matters aside, while keeping them in their proper place and value, I shall propose to you a certain method—as simple as it is excellent—drawn from a certain "double thing" <note>original: *re aliqua bina*. It also falls under our senses in four forms, two visible and two invisible: thunder, wind, hail, and rain. Distill, therefore, a pale and slightly pale water, and then a blue one <note>original: *blaviam*. A medieval Latin term for blue or bluish-gray.</note>; afterward increase the fire, and there follow the most foul-smelling dregs, of whose stench you must beware. Then a smoke follows, by which the oil is condensed in the manner of a dry stone; and if you do not preserve this smoke well, a loss of the work follows.

And thus increase to a good fire, until all the smoke has come out. Afterward let it cool, and at the bottom of the vessel is a black spongy earth. In this way, you have separated the <term>Rebis</term> <note>original: *Rebis*. From the Latin *res bina*, meaning "double thing." The earth ascends into the heaven, which is the <term>alembic</term> <note>original: *alembicum*. A distillation apparatus consisting of two vessels connected by a tube or a cap with a downward-pointing spout.</note>, and the earth is the mother and nurse of the elements. 

Therefore, cast away the earth, as well as the first and second waters, and the most foul-smelling dregs <note>original: *fœces fœtidissimas*. Alchemists often described the waste products of early distillation as having a "stinking" or "corrupt" nature.</note> that follow afterward, which come out before the fire or oil, and take only the fire. Therefore, place this Stone in dung <note>original: *fimum*. A common alchemical practice where the warmth of fermenting horse manure provided a gentle, steady heat for "digestion."</note> in a sealed vessel for seven days; afterward, you shall distill a somewhat clear water from the ashes by a gentle fire, and distill it in this way until all humidity is consumed. 

Bury the distilled water again as before, and distill it seven times in this way, and save the dregs that remain in the distillation. And these dregs are the fire and the <term>Sulfur of the Philosophers</term>. The water, however, is the <term>Mercury of the Philosophers</term> <note>These are the "Sophic" principles—not common sulfur or mercury, but the philosophical essences of stability/combustibility and fluidity/volatility.</note>. And thus you have the four elements: two in the open, and two in the hidden. 

Now take equal parts of each, join them in a glass vessel sealed with lute <note>original: *vitro lutato*. "Lute" was a specialized clay or paste used to hermetically seal glass joints against the pressure of heat.</note> up to the neck, and place a blind alembic <note>original: *cæcumque alembicum*. A distillation head without an outlet tube, used for "refluxing" where vapors rise and fall back into the same vessel.</note> over it with a good seal, placing it...

<summary>This page details the extraction of the alchemical principles of Sulfur and Mercury through repeated distillation and "digestion" in heat. The author emphasizes the need to discard the foul-smelling waste products to isolate the "fire" or oil required for the work.</summary>

<keywords>Hermes Trismegistus, distillation, alembic, Sulfur of the Philosophers, Mercury of the Philosophers, four elements, digestion, lute, laboratory practice</keywords>

<sig>s 2</sig>

...into sifted ashes upon a suitable furnace, administering the fire; then you will see the water rise and fall <note>original: *ascendere & descendere*. This refers to the process of reflux or circulation within the vessel.</note>, and when that vapor has ceased, know that the <term>medicine</term> <note>original: *medicinam*. In alchemy, the "medicine" refers to the substance being refined into the Elixir, which was believed to "heal" base metals into gold or provide health to the human body.</note> is congealed. Break the glass, and take half of the aforesaid water; join it together in another coated vessel <note>original: *vas tunicatum*. A glass vessel reinforced with a "tunic" of clay and hair to protect it from direct heat and prevent shattering.</note> in all ways as before. And this is the **operation for the white** <note>original: *operatio ad album*. Also known as the *Albedo*, this is the second major stage of the Great Work, where the matter is purified to a brilliant white state, capable of transmuting metals into silver.</note>, which is completed in three successions. The first tints <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurium*. Here referring to "Sophic Mercury," the liquid principle of matter.</note>. The second doubles it. The third triples it. Work further according to your understanding, and you will possess the desired result, etc.

<header>THE FIRST OIL.</header>
<meta>catchword: stanci (likely referring to "stanciat" or "calcined" on the following page)</meta>

IN the name of Holy GOD, for the first oil I take:
->*30 pounds of Salt.*<-
->*30 pounds of the best Tartar.* <note>original: *Tartari*. Tartar is the potassium bitartrate crust that forms in wine barrels; it was highly valued in alchemy as a source of "fixed" salt.</note><-
->*5 pounds of the salt of the ashes of oak wood, or beech, or*<-
->*vine branches.*<-
->*Just as much of the salt of quicklime, namely 5 pounds.*<-
->*10 pounds of Sulfur.*<-

I crush and sift these individually, then mix them together carefully, then divide them into equal parts, and place the parts in various glass or glazed pots, into which I soon pour as much of the sharpest vinegar as I see will be necessary. I place my pots on a slow fire, so that they may boil a little, and I stir the matter, so that the sharpness of the vinegar may penetrate more, and extract a salt that is purer and clearly aerial <note>In alchemical theory, an "aerial" substance is one that has been purified of heavy, earthy "grossness" and has become volatile or subtle, like the element of Air.</note>. When, therefore, it has boiled for a little while, namely for half an hour, I allow the matter to rest, so that it may form a sediment; then I pour out that liquid by tilting it into another vessel, and I collect from each vessel all the clear liquid. I distill the collected liquid through the tongues of a filter <note>original: *linguas filtri*. These were strips of cloth or felt acting as wicks to draw liquid through by capillary action, a common method for fine filtration in the early modern laboratory.</note>, so that it comes out clearer and is free of all dregs; soon I place this liquid into various <term>aludels</term> <note>original: *aludelis*. An aludel is a pear-shaped earthenware or glass vessel used as a condenser in the sublimation or distillation process.</note> according to the ratio of the quantity of liquid, on which aludels I place their <term>alembics</term> <note>original: *alembica*. An alembic is the "cap" or still-head of a distilling apparatus that directs vapors into a receiving flask.</note>, and I arrange them in furnaces, and I distill whatever liquid is in the bottom of the aludels; there remains a matter ca- <note>The text cuts off here, likely intending "calcinata" (calcined) or "caput mortuum" (dead head/residue).</note>

<meta>This page continues the specific laboratory instructions for the "First Oil" begun on page 188. The process moves from initial filtration to the creation of a "Philosophical Salt" and its subsequent transformation through varying degrees of heat.</meta>

...tin-colored, which easily becomes greasy to the touch and melts: a material I call **Philosophical Salt** <note>original: *Salem Philosophicum*. In alchemy, this is not common table salt, but a purified, "fixed" substance that has been stripped of its impurities and is capable of resisting fire.</note>. Meanwhile, I prepare distilled vinegar for myself, through which I dissolve it again into a liquid.

Having done this, I find this salt has taken the form of olive oil. To this oil, I add twice its weight of good thrice-distilled vinegar, and I distill it again through **alembics** <note>An alembic is the "cap" or hood of a still, used to catch vapors and direct them into a cooling vessel.</note>. Finally, I pour over it twice its weight of **water of life** <note>original: *aqvæ vitæ*. This usually refers to highly concentrated alcohol, prized by alchemists as a "universal" solvent for extracting the "spirit" of a substance.</note>, which we call the power of the **Fifth Essence** <note>original: *quintam essentiam*. The "quintessence" was the purest, most spiritual element beyond the four earthly elements (earth, air, fire, water), believed to hold life-giving properties.</note>, and I congeal it again through distillation. Once all moisture has evaporated through this final distillation, I increase the fire by one degree, and then by another, so that the oily matter turns to stone. In turn, I increase the fire further so that it hardens completely into a stone.

I crush this stone very quickly and place it upon a stone for dissolving <note>This likely refers to the process of "deliquescence," where a chemical salt is placed on a marble slab or in a damp cellar to absorb moisture from the air until it spontaneously melts into a liquid "oil."</note>, so that it may spontaneously resolve back into an oil. This oil excels all sugar tenfold in sweetness. I collect it most carefully in glass dishes, and having collected it all, I congeal it again by fire into a stone. I place that stone, once crushed, into a good **cucurbit** <note>A gourd-shaped glass vessel that forms the base of a still where the matter is heated.</note>, and I commit it first to a slower fire. To the cucurbit, I add a large and wide glass receiving vessel. For twelve hours I maintain a moderate fire, and then I gradually increase it by degrees until the cucurbit begins to glow white-hot:

...then I remove the receiving vessel, and if any liquid has distilled, I throw it away. I then attach the receiving vessel again to the mouth of the **cucurbit** <note>A gourd-shaped glass vessel used in distillation.</note>, and I seal the joints with the best **lute** <note>original: *luto*. A paste or cement used to make vessels airtight to prevent the escape of precious vapors.</note>. It is necessary to act cautiously here, for these **spirits** <note>In alchemy, "spirits" refers to the volatile gases or vapors released during heating.</note> are too fierce to want to subside unless one acts with care. Therefore, during the first surges, one must be extremely vigilant until they settle and are resolved into an oil. Finally, the spirits are not so fierce, but they settle into oil more quickly than the earlier ones did.

The sight is truly delightful: I increase the fire more and more continuously for three days, as if I wished to melt copper or iron. At last, I collect my holy and truly divine oil, which surpasses the **ruby** in color and sugar in sweetness; so much so that if a single drop of this oil is poured into a quart of water, it converts it into the most pleasant and sweetest flavor, and it assists wine in a wonderful way. 

And this is that true **balsam**, sought by so many. Who could say how much it is worth for curing illnesses? Or who could narrate its virtues? This is the basis of true **potable gold** <note>original: *auri potabilis*. A legendary alchemical elixir made of gold dissolved in liquid, believed by Renaissance physicians to be a "universal medicine" capable of curing all diseases and granting longevity.</note>. This is that true **myrrh**, which we rightly ought to offer to Christ our Redeemer and Savior. 

Regarding these virtues, I shall write more extensively at another time to **Your Sacred Royal Majesty** <note>original: *V. S. R. M.* (Vestrae Sacrae Regiae Maiestati). This indicates the manuscript was a report or dedication to a high-ranking patron, possibly a King or Emperor.</note>. For our undertaking, it is necessary that I have a good quantity of this oil, which oil I keep in a glass vessel diligently for [various] uses...

# THE SECOND OIL.

For the second oil I take:
*   **Live Sulfur** <note>original: *Sulphuris vivi*. Native or elemental sulfur, often considered the "soul" of metals.</note>: 10 lbs.
*   **Tartar**: 10 ½ lbs. <note>original: *lb. 10 β*. The symbol "β" or "ss" in alchemical recipes denotes *semis*, meaning one-half.</note>
*   **Saltpeter** <note>original: *Salis petræ*. Potassium nitrate, a powerful oxidizing agent.</note>: 5 lbs.
*   **Sublimated Quicksilver** <note>original: *Argenti vivi fublimati*. Mercury that has been purified through sublimation, often resulting in mercuric chloride.</note>: 3 lbs.
*   **Sal Ammoniac**: 5 lbs.

I mix these together after they have been crushed and sifted, and just as was done above with the first oil, I make this second oil using that same process and regimen. This is the oil that they truly call the **Oil of the Philosophers**. For besides this fire, we find no other that acts upon **our earth**—that is, upon gold; this is the truly **elemental fire** <note>original: *ignis elementalis*. In alchemy, this refers to a "liquid fire" or a potent solvent capable of breaking down substances at a molecular level.</note>. Under no circumstances are medicines for the human body to be prepared from this oil; rather, it is for healing the "infirmities" of metals and gemstones <note>The "infirmities of metals" refers to the alchemical belief that base metals were "sick" versions of gold and could be "cured" or transmuted into perfection.</note>. 

Likewise, I preserve this oil in a well-sealed glass vessel for use; I have need of a good quantity of this for our works. Once these two oils have been procured, I proceed as follows: I take three pounds of each oil and join them together in a large glass vessel with great caution, lest they boil over; for as soon as they touch one another, they immediately rouse their fires, and there becomes a great...

Soon after, I pour over it simple <term>strong water</term><note>original: *aquam fortem*. I repeat this process again and again until I have ten pounds of this water. Once the water is collected, I place it—just like the others—for <term>circulation</term><note>original: *circulandum*. A process where a liquid is distilled and then allowed to flow back into the same vessel in a continuous cycle, thought to "purify" or "invigorate" the substance over time.</note>. This circulation of the water can be done at the same time as the circulation of the other substances so that we may save time. Once the circulation is finished, this water will be more red than can be described; its power is marvelous for many things, especially for creating precious gemstones.

When all the above-mentioned things have been circulated, in the name of **GOD Most High**, I take the gold that was circulated first, to which I join the circulated silver, and to these...

To these two I add 10 lb. of <term>mercurial water</term><note>original: *aquæ mercurialis*. A specialized alchemical solvent or "water" derived from mercury, used to dissolve or transform metals.</note>, stirring well; thus I join them and place them again to <term>circulate</term><note>original: *circulandum*. A process where a substance is heated so that it evaporates and then condenses back into the same vessel, "purifying" it through repeated cycles.</note> for 21 days. When these have elapsed, I open the vessel and increase the fire for three continuous days, so that they may be well-fused and joined proportionally, so that there may be an indissoluble marriage of nature, and that <term>celestial heroes</term><note>original: *heroes cœlestes*. Highly potent alchemical substances or "spirits" resulting from the successful union of base elements.</note> might be procreated for us. 

Good God, preserve us, so that we may celebrate this, Your holy marriage. Countless authors have written of this marriage of Nature and divine union; whether they understood it, I cannot say—at the very least, I know very few have understood it. Here first is founded the <term>Philosophers’ Stone</term><note>original: *Lapis Philosophorum*. The legendary goal of alchemy, believed to transform base metals into gold and grant spiritual or physical perfection.</note>.

->Here *NATURE* ends,<-
->now *ART* begins.<-

These things being done, I have ready two small glass vessels, which we call <term>dissolving vessels</term><note>original: *vascula dissolutoria*.</note>, and I take this glorious stone and carefully divide it. I take 4 ounces of it and place it into the aforementioned vessel, to which I add sixteen ounces of <term>philosophical oil</term><note>original: *oleo Philosophico*. Also called "spermate" (seed) in the text, this is a refined alchemical substance thought to contain the life-force of the metal.</note> or seed, and I place it over warm ashes for three continuous hours. If I wish to prepare more vessels in this way, I can, provided I maintain the proportion of all things. Here is to be seen a marvelous secret of Nature, for the gold is stripped of its body and is resolved into its own <term>quicksilver</term><note>original: *argentum vivum*. Mercury; in this context, the "essential" or "living" mercury of the gold itself.</note>, which shines as if it were a star,

<summary>This page describes the final "circulation" and heating required to create the foundation of the Philosophers' Stone, marking a transition from natural preparation to the alchemist's active "Art." The author uses the metaphor of a "holy marriage" to describe the chemical bonding of materials and the resulting transformation of gold into a brilliant, star-like mercurial form.</summary>

May the holy Lord God, glorious forever and ever, preserve Your Royal Majesty, so that you may deign to see this great mystery of God, and may He lead my life at least until that time when I might humbly manifest this mystery for the glory of His holy name to Your Serene Royal Majesty. Amen.

A term used by alchemists to describe the darkening of the materials as they react, symbolizing the "death" of the old forms.</note> of our Nature will last for about four hours; finally, they will come together into one body and hide themselves in their own ashes, like that unique and divine <term>Phoenix</term><note>The mythical bird that burns and rises from its own ashes, representing the alchemical cycle of destruction and rebirth.</note>.

# LOWER ASTRONOMY
## OR THE MOTION AND
### VARIATION OF THE
### TERRESTRIAL PLANETS.
<note>original: *Astronomia Inferior, seu Planetarum Terrestrium Motus*. "Lower Astronomy" is a common Renaissance term for alchemy, as it studies the "planets" or metals within the earth, mirroring the "Upper Astronomy" of the stars.</note>

The rule of the <term>Lords of the houses</term><note>original: *Dominorum domorum*. In astrology, a planet "ruling" a house has primary influence there; in alchemy, this refers to the dominant chemical or spiritual principle acting upon a substance at a specific stage.</note> and of <term>exaltation</term><note>original: *exaltationis*. A state where a planet’s power is at its peak. Alchemically, this describes the process of raising a substance to a higher level of purity or potency.</note> is this: The Lord of the house exalts the Lord of exaltation in color and elements to a higher state, and leads it to perfection. That is, the Lord of exaltation is improved, and not the lord of his own house. Example: Thus the Sun by Mars, and the Moon by Venus, etc.; and this according to its special property implanted in it from the qualities of the **Zodiac** belonging to it.

<summary>The author concludes a letter to the Queen regarding the "holy business" of alchemy and begins a new section defining "Lower Astronomy," using astrological terminology to explain how metals (terrestrial planets) are perfected through specific relationships.</summary>
<keywords>London 1565, Queen Elizabeth I, Lower Astronomy, Terrestrial Planets, Alchemical Astrology, Exaltation, Lords of the Houses</keywords>

--- Page 306 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the general rules of "Lower Astronomy" (alchemy) to a specific operation involving the Sun (gold) and Mars (iron), specifically within the astrological sign of Aries.</meta>

The preparation of this Lord <note>original: *Domini-mus*. Likely a scribal error or contraction for *Domini* (Lord) or *Dominorum* (Lords), referring to the planetary rulers mentioned on the previous page.</note> is such according to the quality of the **right-hand fire**, and the nature of the **left-hand** from its summit. And this is the most certain and infallible experience. There are four natures in the world, and four in the heavens. And they fulfill every boundary; they exalt and they depress, according to the mixture of the Mystery, and thus this preparation is made perfect.

He who wishes to **exalt** the <term>earthly Sun</term><note>original: *Solem terrenum*. In the language of alchemy, the "earthly Sun" is gold, the most perfect of the seven metals.</note>, to lead it to a red color and to raise it, and to make the **cooked bronze of the Philosophers** <note>original: *æs Philosophorum coctum*. A symbolic name for a purified metallic base that has undergone "digestion" or heating.</note>, which may further <term>tinge</term><note>original: *tingat*. To "tinge" is to fundamentally change the color and nature of a base metal, a key step in transmutation.</note> the remaining bodies—and especially change the **Moon** <note>Silver</note> into the **Sun** <note>Gold</note>—let him proceed in this way without any deceit.

**The Sun is exalted in the house of Mars.** <note>In traditional astrology, the Sun is "exalted" (reaches its peak power) in Aries, which is the "house" or sign ruled by the planet Mars.</note> The Sun is the **Lord of exaltation**; that is, the gold to be exalted. Mars, however, is the **Lord of the house**, who performs the exalting. Therefore, the <term>tincture of Mars</term><note>original: *tinctura Martis*. The refined essence or chemical extract of iron.</note> elevates the Sun to the aforementioned higher state, and no other of the Planets can do so. Since, therefore, **Aries** is a fiery sign, **Mars** first must either be <term>reverberated</term><note>original: *reverberandus*. Subjected to intense, indirect heat in a reverberatory furnace to cause a chemical change.</note> by fire into a most subtle <term>saffron</term><note>original: *crocum*. A fine, usually reddish metallic powder or oxide, such as *Crocus Martis* (iron oxide).</note>; or be <term>calcined</term><note>original: *calcinandus*. Burned to a powder or ash.</note> by some fiery mineral.

It asserts that the "tincture" of Mars is the only substance capable of exalting Gold to its highest, "red" state for the purpose of transmuting silver.</summary>

**Note: The Sign**

->**Fire** <note>original: *Igneum*.</note> signifies **fire**.<-
->**Water** <note>original: *Aqueum*.</note> signifies **dissolution** <note>original: *solutionem*. The process of turning a solid into a liquid state.</note>.<-
->**Air** <note>original: *Aëreum*.</note> signifies **sublimation** <note>original: *sublimationem*. A process where a solid is vaporized and then solidified again to purify it.</note>.<-
->**Earth** <note>original: *Terreum*.</note> signifies **congealing** <note>original: *congelationem*.</note> and reduction to **powder**.<-

**Aries**, **Leo**, **Sagittarius**: Fire signs; hot and dry.
**Taurus**, **Virgo**, **Capricorn**: Earth signs; cold and dry.
**Gemini**, **Libra**, **Aquarius**: Air signs; hot and moist.
**Cancer**, **Scorpio**, **Pisces**: Water signs; cold and moist.

Thus, if you wish to **exalt Luna** <note>To "exalt" a metal is to raise it to a higher state of purity or to transform it into a "nobler" metal like gold.</note> to the degree of **Sol**, it is done in this way: **Venus** <note>Copper</note> is the exaltation of **Luna** <note>Silver</note>. Gemini represents the "Air" operation of sublimation.</note>; that is, **sublimate** it.

Afterwards, dissolve [the substance] through **Cancer** <note>original: *cancrum*. To turn a liquid or dissolved substance back into a solid form.</note> them; you will then have **Gold** <note>original: *Solem*. This is most true and certain.

**Tin** <note>original: *Jupiter*.</note> is very easily exalted by **Silver**. Prepare the Silver through **Gemini**, and bring it back to the nature of **Cancer**; it then draws the Tin to itself, so that they are not separated. Therefore, let there be a sublimation <note>original: *sublimatio*. However, these specific instructions likely come from the "Pseudo-Geber," a 13th-century European chemist whose works were highly influential in the Middle Ages.</note> intends when he says: 
> Silver hardens Tin and converts it to itself, etc.

**Lead** <note>original: *Saturnus*.</note> exalts **Iron** <note>original: *Martem*.</note>. A "tincture" in alchemy is a substance capable of "dyeing" or transforming the color and nature of a metal.</note>.

**Tin** <note>original: *Jupiter*.</note> exalts **Copper** <note>original: *Venerem*.</note>. Therefore, prepare the Tin according to the nature of **Virgo**, then **Libra**, and finally **Scorpio**, and then apply the tincture.

**Mercury exalts itself.** Because its exaltation is in its own house <note>original: *propria domo*. In astrology, a "house" or "home" is a zodiac sign where a planet has its strongest influence; for Mercury, this is traditionally Virgo.</note>. Therefore the Philosophers <note>original: *Philosophi*. Alchemists often referred to themselves and their predecessors as "Philosophers."</note> speak truly, saying: Mercury kills itself, brings itself to life, and exalts itself. And its exaltation happens in this way: let it be governed for a long time in a vessel of precipitation <note>original: *vase præcipitationis*. A laboratory container used to allow solids to settle out of a liquid solution.</note>, until it is turned into a most red powder. Let this powder be sublimated <note>original: *sublimetur*. The process of heating a substance into vapor and then cooling it back into a solid to purify it.</note> and dissolved into a most red water, in which **Silver** <note>original: *Luna*. Literally "the Moon," the alchemical name for silver.</note> is frequently quenched; it will undoubtedly be tinged with the color of **Gold** <note>original: *Solis*. Literally "the Sun," the alchemical name for gold.</note>. Or, let it be congealed and cast upon Silver, and it becomes Gold.

**Taurus, Virgo, and Capricorn** regard each other with a trine aspect <note>original: *aspectu trino*. An astrological relationship where signs are 120 degrees apart, considered a harmonious and powerful alignment.</note>, whose lords are **Copper** <note>original: *Venus*. The planet Venus governs copper in the alchemical system.</note>, **Mercury**, and **Lead** <note>original: *Saturnus*. The planet Saturn governs lead.</note>. I therefore join equal parts <note>original: *ana*. A term used in recipes meaning "of each an equal amount."</note> of the filings of Copper and **Steel** <note>original: *Chalybis*.</note>. And because these are southern signs, calcine <note>original: *calcina*. To heat a substance to high temperatures in order to oxidize it or reduce it to a powder.</note> them with **arsenic**. Afterwards, wash the amalgam <note>original: *amalgama*. A mixture or alloy of metals, often involving mercury.</note> of Mercury with Lead, mix them, create layer upon layer <note>original: *stratum super stratum*. A technique of stacking alternating materials in a crucible.</note>, and melt them; you will have a brittle material. To this, join a fourth part of Silver, and test it in the cupel <note>original: *cineritio*. A porous vessel used to refine precious metals by absorbing impurities.</note>, and you will see the **gift of God** <note>original: *donum DEI*. A common alchemical expression for the successful creation of a precious metal.</note>.

**Lead** <note>original: *Saturnus*.</note> reduces both luminaries <note>original: *ambo luminaria*. Referring to the Sun (Gold) and the Moon (Silver).</note> to the **prime matter** <note>original: *primam materiam*. The original, formless substance from which all things were thought to be created.</note>, because it is opposed

In those whose Lords of the houses are opposites, the superior always conquers the inferior. Therefore **Lead** conquers **Silver** <note>original: *Lunam*.</note> by melting, and the smoke of **Lead** breaks **Gold** into a calx <note>original: *calcem*. A powder produced by heating a metal, representing its "death" or reduction to its base components.</note>. And note, that **Lead** is destroyed by the sphere <note>original: *coelum*. Literally "heaven," referring to the astrological influence or "sky" of the planet.</note> of **Silver**. And understand the same for other opposites, just as many ants gathered together may conquer a serpent. The greater conquers the great, and the great conquers the lesser.

**Tin** <note>original: *Jupiter*.</note> conquers **Mercury** when dissolved by means of **sal ammoniac** <note>original: *Sale armoniaco*. Ammonium chloride, a pungent salt used as a powerful solvent in the lab.</note>, and sublimated **Mercury** soaked in it fixes <note>original: *fixat*. The process of making a volatile substance solid or resistant to heat.</note> it very quickly. Understand it thus: **Tin** is first dissolved by itself in a cellar <note>original: *cellario*. Alchemists used damp cellars to allow salts to naturally absorb moisture from the air and turn into liquid.</note>, and the **Mercury** is softened <note>original: *inceratus*. And then, when both are dissolved and coagulated, they most truly tinge <note>original: *tingunt*. To change the color or nature of a metal, a key step in transmutation.</note> **Copper** <note>original: *Venerem*.</note> into **Silver**.

The superior fixes the inferior (among those whose houses are opposite), and thus it appears that **Lead** fixes **Silver**, because it conquers it. Therefore **Lead** dissolved by means...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the chemical transformation and "fixing" of metals, building on the previous discussion of planetary sympathies. (206.)

...with **sal ammoniac** <note>original: *armoniaco sale*. Ammonium chloride, used to dissolve and clean metals.</note>, and **Silver** <note>original: *Luna*. Literally "The Moon," the alchemical name for silver.</note> calcined <note>original: *calcinata*. Heated until reduced to a powder.</note> and soaked with it, and reduced three times, and placed in the **"Bed of the Philosophers"** <note>original: *lecto Philosophorum*. A technical term for a layered arrangement of materials in a crucible.</note> (that is, layer upon layer with some cementing powder), the **Silver** will be fixed by the addition of only a small amount of **Gold** <note>original: *auro*. Alchemists "fixed" metals to make them stable and resistant to heat, a step toward transmutation.</note>, and it will stand in judgment <note>original: *stabit in judicio*. It will pass the official tests of purity, such as fire assays.</note>.

**Iron** <note>original: *Mars*.</note> essentially joined with **Copper** <note>original: *Venere*. Literally "Venus."</note> in equal parts <note>original: *in ana*. A pharmaceutical and alchemical term meaning "of each an equal amount."</note>, because their [astrological] houses are equally distant, it conquers it by thickening and fixing it. Likewise, melt **Iron** four times by means of **arsenic**; afterwards, place it under glass in a glassmaker’s furnace until it becomes malleable, and let it be joined to **Silver**; then it will not be separated from it forever. And this is beyond all other experiments.

**Tin** <note>original: *Jupiter*.</note> aspects Aquarius in a sextile <note>An astrological aspect of 60 degrees, considered harmonious.</note> by means of Capricorn from a fiery sign. Therefore, **Tin** calcined and soaked in dissolved **Lead** <note>original: *Saturno soluto*.</note>, and then reduced, loses the greatest of its "vices" <note>original: *vitiis*. In alchemy, "vices" refer to the undesirable properties of a metal, such as Tin's "cry" or its brittleness.</note>. The powder of reduction for **Tin** is as follows: **Take** <note>original: *R*. The symbol for *Recipe*, meaning "take this."</note> 1 part soot, 2 parts common salt, and cook it in "the East" <gloss>that is, urine</gloss> in an amount equal to the soot. Afterward, add to it one part **saltpeter** <note>original: *Salis nitri*.</note> and one part **calcined tartar** <note>original: *tartari calcinati*. Potassium carbonate produced by heating the sediment from wine barrels.</note>; grind them all together.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for chemical transmutation, moving from the stabilization of metals to the "sympathetic" relationships between planetary metals. It explains how certain metals "perfect" or dissolve others based on their perceived astrological and physical affinities.</meta>

(207.)

T 4

Take two parts of this recipe to one part of the **body** <note>original: *corporis*. In alchemy, "the body" usually refers to the base metal being acted upon.</note> to be reduced.

Always the superior planet helps the inferior. In this way, **Lead** <note>original: *Saturnus*.</note> removes the "screeching" of **Tin** <note>original: *stridorem Jovis*. Known as the "tin cry," this is the crackling sound pure tin makes when bent; alchemists believed adding lead "cured" this.</note>. **Tin** <note>original: *Jupiter*.</note> melts **Iron** <note>original: *Martem*.</note>. **Iron** colors **Gold** <note>original: *Solem*. Literally "the Sun."</note>. **Gold** perfects **Copper** <note>original: *Venerem*. Literally "Venus."</note>. **Copper** perfects **Quicksilver** <note>original: *Mercurium*.</note>, and **Quicksilver** perfects **Silver** <note>original: *Lunam*. Literally "the Moon."</note>.

**Copper** also, calcined <note>original: *calcinata*. Reduced to powder by heat.</note> by means of **arsenic** and washed until pure, should be soaked with **Tin** that has been distilled through an **alembic** <note>A chemical still used for distillation.</note>, until there is a sufficient amount, and then it may be reduced into true **Silver**.

Let a "water" <note>original: *aqua*. Usually refers to an acid or a solvent.</note> be made from **Lead** and **Quicksilver** in the usual manner. This dissolves **Silver**, because **Lead** is a friend to **Silver**. But **Tin** hates **Silver** and is friendly to **Gold**. Therefore, it also dissolves **Gold**. For this reason, dissolve **Gold** in the "water" of **Tin**, and join it with **Silver** dissolved in the "water" of **Lead**, and you will have honor <note>i.e., you will achieve a successful result.</note>. The operation will succeed even better if you also add calcined **Iron** that has been dissolved in the "water" of **Tin**.

Likewise, pour rectified <note>Purified by repeated distillation.</note> "water" of **Tin** over **Silver** that has been dissolved in ***aqua fortis*** <note>Nitric acid; literally "strong water."</note>, and let it be drawn off until it becomes an oil; digest this for a day and a night, then distill it.

### *Here Ends the Lower Astronomy.* <note>original: *Inferior Astronomia*. Alchemists often called their craft "Lower Astronomy" because they believed the metals on Earth corresponded to the planets in the heavens.</note>

> If you wish through God's grace alone

> Seek it not in herbs or animals,

> Of this **Chaos**, without fail;

Then the animated spirit dissolves the body /

Its own blood makes it young /
When it takes the **ninth drink** / <note>In alchemy, "drinks" often refer to the number of times a substance is moistened with a solvent or distilled to increase its potency.</note>
After every drink it flies /
And finally remains snow-white /
It also often turns black, red, and yellow /
Yet there is no hiding of the colors /
Until it shines clear, white, and pure /
Much clearer than fine crystals /
Rightly candied, cubed, not hard, yet bright /
Its own soul makes it so clear
By means of the spirit / Yet not hastily /
In several months it will be completed /
And it is truly the right **ferment** <term>fermentum</term> for certain / <note>The "ferment" is the substance that acts like yeast, capable of "infecting" and transforming other metals into its own perfected nature.</note>
Preserve this treasure with high diligence.
It will, without mockery and truly /
Be accomplished through water and earth.

<summary>This page uses poetic metaphors like the Raven and the Swan to describe the purification of the alchemical matter through repeated distillation. It concludes with the creation of a "ferment" that has been perfected over several months.</summary>

## Summary Content of the
### Little Book, called *Gloria Mundi* <note>original: "Gloria Mundi" (The Glory of the World). This was a famous alchemical treatise first published in the early 17th century.</note>, or Paradise Tablets, gathered together into short Propositions <term>positiones</term> and Concordances,
->as follows:<-

->I.<-

1. If you wish to obtain from God wisdom and understanding, grace and blessing for the preparation of the ancient **Stone of the Wise** <term>Lapis Philosophorum</term>, then ask Him for it from the heart, with a true Christian intention <term>intent</term> to use such a gift of His rightly. <note>In traditional alchemy, spiritual purity and divine permission were considered more important than laboratory equipment.</note>

2. Therefore, guard yourself against the **vulgar alchemy** <term>Alchymisterey</term> of the common rabble, which works in many things and with many things <note>A warning against "puffers"—alchemists who tried to make gold using dozens of random, often foul, ingredients rather than following the single "Natural" path.</note>, and contemplate with all diligence the beginning of all natural things—namely, what process <term>proceß</term> and order God the Lord maintained in the **creation of the world**.

3. For just as in the beginning there was one **Matter** <term>materia</term>, called *Hyle* <note>original Greek: "Hyle," referring to the primordial, formless "first matter" from which the universe was shaped.</note>, which was neither earth nor water, neither air nor fire; it was not light, nor was it dark:

<summary>This page introduces a summary of the "Gloria Mundi," emphasizing that the secret to the Philosophers' Stone lies in divine grace and mimicking the original process of God's creation of the universe from primordial matter.</summary>
<keywords>Gloria Mundi, Paradise Tablets, Philosophers' Stone, Hyle, First Matter, Creation of the World, Christian intention, vulgar alchemy</keywords>

likeness/but a confused mixture of all these: Thus also the **First Matter** <term>prima materia</term> <note>original: "prima materia" — the primal, formless substance that alchemists believed was the starting point for creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note> of our Stone is nothing more than just one thing, composed of many things through the order of God, and not by human hands; for it is a fire, and yet has no form of fire; it is air, and has no appearance of air; it is water, and yet no other water is equal to it; it is earth, and yet has no form of earth. Learn not only to rightly recognize this thing, but also to know its meaning.

4. This thing or **matter** <term>materia</term> is to be found in no place in the whole wide world, neither within nor upon the ground/ and yet it is nonetheless easily and sufficiently obtained/ for free/ without any cost/ and for that reason it is bought for money in no shop/ nor is it dug out of wild or tame mountains with pickaxes and other instruments/ it is found in no animal/ in no herb/ in any mineral or metallic thing/ not in **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurio"</note>, or **Copper** <note>original: "Venere" — Venus was the alchemical name for copper.</note>, nor in any salt that was ever found in and upon the earth/ in the sea and the water.

If you do not learn the **matter** <term>materia</term> of our **Stone** from this report, then you will never recognize it. Herewith let the lover of this art be commended to **GOD** the Almighty in eternity. Given after Christ's birth, Anno 1526.

8. Now we proceed to the preparation of the **Stone**: Just as **God** the Almighty has created all things out of dryness and moisture, so it also happens in this work. Therefore, in the name of the Lord, take the aforementioned **matter** <term>materia</term> wherein our **Stone** is contained; put it into a flask <note>German: *Colben*</note>, put a head <note>an alembic or still-head used to capture vapor</note> upon it, place a receiver <note>German: *Fürlag*; a vessel to collect the distillate</note> before it, set it in the water bath <term>B. Then grind the **body** <term>Corpus</term> <note>the solid residue left after distillation</note> small, and set it back in the water bath <term>B. M.</term> and leave it in the heat until it dissolves <note>original: *solvirt*</note>; then give it its water back to drink <note>The process of "feeding" the solid matter with its own distilled liquid is known as "imbibition."</note>, all bit by bit, from time to time, until it becomes beautiful and clear. The distilled water is the **spirit** <term>Spiritus</term>, which [is given] to the dead **body** <term>Corpus</term>

...should be praised and honored, and that man should serve his neighbor in all his needs and concerns; therefore, take good care that your talent <note>original: *Pfund*; literally "pound," a reference to the biblical Parable of the Talents where a servant is punished for burying his money rather than investing it.</note> does not remain buried, but trade with it for the honor of God and the service of your neighbor. Instead, you should reveal and share it with the true lovers <note>original: *Liebhabern*; in this context, devoted students or practitioners of the alchemical Art.</note> and the needy, the God-fearing, pious, and charitable people, as they are the ones who honor such gifts of God, use them rightly, and know how to keep them hidden.

But should this book of mine ever fall into the hands of the godless, then may God strike them with blindness and foolishness, so that they neither understand nor grasp it, much less be able to work <note>original: *laboriren*; from the Latin *laborare*, referring to performing operations in a laboratory.</note> on it and bring it to use. With this wish, I intend to have this, my Paradise Table <note>original: *ParadeißTafel*; likely a poetic title for the manuscript or a specific summary diagram representing the culmination of the Great Work.</note>, sealed and closed, etc.

# Following is a singular work, which

arises from a certain ethereal Central Salt <note>original: *Centrali quodam Sale æthereo*; alchemists believed a "central salt" was the fundamental seed or spirit within all matter.</note> resolved in an earthly mineral fire, or oil of vitriol <term>oil of vitriol: concentrated sulfuric acid, one of the most important solvents used by early chemists to break down metals</term>, and then fermented with the extracted tincture of the Sun <note>original: *tincturâ Solis extractâ*; the "Sun" is the alchemical cipher for gold; its tincture was believed to be its medicinal essence.</note>, matured externally and boiled out with Solar-Olympic fire <note>original: *igne Solympico*; likely referring to the use of burning mirrors or lenses to concentrate natural sunlight.</note>, or the invisible
->fire of the rays of the sun, from the<-
->*Theophrastus Reborn* <note>original: *Theophrasto redivivo*; a reference to the revival of the teachings of Paracelsus (Theophrastus von Hohenheim).</note> of Michael Pezel<-
->near the end.<-

<summary>The author concludes his moral exhortations by praying that the unworthy remain "blind" to the book's secrets before introducing a new procedure for creating a solar medicine. This process involves "Central Salt," sulfuric acid, and the concentrated heat of the sun's rays.</summary>

<keywords>Paradise Table, secrecy, Central Salt, oil of vitriol, Tincture of the Sun, Solar-Olympic fire, Michael Pezel, Paracelsianism</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the specific alchemical process introduced on the previous page, moving from the theoretical nature of the "Central Salt" to a highly allegorical recipe involving the "Virgin" and the "Lion" to produce the Mercury of the Philosophers.</meta>

<page-num>(225.)</page-num>

Whoever wishes to prepare the right, true
<term>Tincture of the Philosophers: the legendary "Philosopher's Stone" in liquid form, capable of transmuting metals and perfecting the human body</term>,
let him take a beautiful virgin born of
royal lineage <note>original: *Jungfrau aus Königlichem Stamm*; an allegorical reference to a pure mineral substance, likely Gold or highly purified Antimony, which alchemists considered "noble."</note>, who was conceived in the
Sun and grown young in the
Lion <note>original: *Löwen*; "The Lion" often refers to the zodiac sign Leo, representing the heat of summer, or to the "Green Lion," a code name for strong mineral acids like vitriol</note>, and strip the clothes from her
body, and bathe her most beautifully; afterwards take
sharp and distilled vinegar <note>original: *Essig*; in alchemy, this is not household vinegar but "Philosophical Vinegar," a potent acidic solvent used to break down minerals</note>, as the
true Philosophers well know, of
this make another bath and place the virgin
in it, so she falls asleep <note>This "sleep" describes the substance dissolving into the liquid solvent</note>. Meanwhile, the
sharp vinegar extracts from all her
limbs the <term>Quintessence: the "fifth essence" or the hidden, spiritual core of a substance that remains after the four physical elements are stripped away</term>,
which settles at the bottom (like
crystals or saltpeter). When this
is observed, let the virgin
climb out of the bath again and return
to her mother from whence she came;
it harms neither her health nor her honor <note>This indicates that the base material can be recovered or reused, as only its "essence" has been taken</note>.
The bath is placed upon hot ashes and
left to evaporate, and thus you will find the Quin-
tessence lying at the bottom, in color
like honey, heavy in weight; and you
truly have the <term>Mercury of the Philosophers: the "universal spirit" or the essential liquid principle of all metals</term>,
or the salt of metals <note>original: *sal metallorum*</note>.

Afterwards, take a beautiful youth <note>original: *Jüngling*; likely representing a volatile mineral spirit, often associated with sulfur or silver, which must be combined with the "Virgin" (Mercury)</note>,

water <note>original: *künstlichem Wasser*; a refined liquid essence or solvent</note>, and pour it over the <term>Quintessence: the "fifth essence," the spiritual core of a substance separated from the four physical elements</term> <note>original: *Quintam essentiam*</note>

<term>philosophical days: not literal 24-hour days, but symbolic periods of time or stages of the heating process</term>.

and they bow to the king, who

<summary>This page describes the "Chemical Marriage," where the essence of a "Youth" (the King's heart) is joined with the "Virgin's" essence in a sealed vessel. Through a period of "philosophical sleep," the substance is transformed into a perfected "Newborn King," representing the successful creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>

<meta>This page transitions from the successful chemical union described on page 226 to a new allegorical section. Having established the "Newborn King," the text now introduces a judicial or "martial" allegory—a common trope in alchemical literature where various metals (represented by gods/planets) undergo a legal or military conflict to determine their purity and rank.</meta>

<page-num>(227.)</page-num>
<meta>catchword: The</meta>

# The Judgment or Settlement of the God and Judge MERCURY,
## In the ancient War of the Knights / upon
->the complaint and answer of Sol and Mars, clearly set before the eyes in the form of paintings / and shared with me by a trusted, good friend.<-

Although I was determined to conclude and complete this book with paintings and figures, just as it was begun with <term>symbolic plates: engraved illustrations used to convey complex chemical processes through mythological imagery</term> <note>original: *tabulis typicis*</note>: I have nonetheless had to set this aside due to concerns over the slow production of the printing blocks <note>original: *Formen*; likely woodcuts or copper engraving plates</note>. May the friendly reader, therefore, be favorably content with the mere words, as the content of such paintings is sufficiently contained and indicated therein; and they proceed as follows:

### The First Painting.

A warrior is hewn into pieces lengthwise; beneath it is written: The power of the earthly king is gone; his commander <note>original: *Feldhauptmann*; in alchemy, this often represents a primary mineral agent or a catalyst</note>, a kinsman of the king and a very valiant hero in battle, is overcome.

<summary>The author introduces an allegorical "War of the Knights" involving Mercury, Sol, and Mars, apologizing for the lack of actual illustrations due to printing delays. The first described image depicts a defeated warrior, symbolizing the destruction or "death" of a base metal before its transformation.</summary>
<keywords>Mercury, Sol, Mars, War of the Knights, alchemical allegory, printing blocks, symbolic plates, transformation</keywords>

--- Page 338 ---
<meta>This page continues the allegorical description of the "War of the Knights" introduced on the previous page. Having established the defeat of the "earthly king" and his commander, the text now describes the subsequent stages of their "punishment"—a metaphor for the chemical processes of calcination, dissolution, and distillation.</meta>

<page-num>228</page-num>
<meta>catchword: The</meta>

## The Second Painting.

Here is a warrior <note>In alchemical symbolism, a warrior or knight typically represents Mars (Iron) or the "martial" state of a metal undergoing transformation.</note> dismembered and hanging from a gallows, surrounded by a wall; beneath him is a sea, and a fiery man who spits out fire so that the sea begins to steam. Below is written:

> Through my fiery power, I have prepared a salty bath <note>original: *gesaltzen Bad*; likely a saline solution or acid used to dissolve the metal</note> of two duelists <note>original: *Fechtern*; this refers to two opposing chemical agents reacting together</note> for the strong hero; furthermore, because of the misdeed he committed, he shall choke while hanging until the rose-colored marrow <note>original: *Rosenfarbe Marck*; a poetic term for the red essence or "tincture" that emerges during the final stages of the work</note> has completely pressed out of his strong legs.

## The Third Painting.

There stands one with a cup in hand, throwing an eagle <note>The "eagle" is a common alchemical symbol for volatility or corrosive acids (such as "the eagle of the philosophers") that "consume" the fixed metal.</note> into it; beside it is written: 

> The sweetness of the grapevine <note>The grapevine often refers to the "spirit of wine" or a specific solvent used in the process.</note> is gone; its adversary has triumphed, so that through the power of the eagle, the blood may be sucked out of the marrow of the combatant.

## The Fourth Painting.

There the eagle flies quite wet out of a vessel, and a red earth remains behind; by the eagle is written: 

> Alas, I am in pain that I should be robbed of my royal food.

By the red earth stands: 

> See, my form has become like Adam <note>In alchemical thought, "Adam" is frequently linked to the Hebrew word *Adama*, meaning "red earth." This signifies the return of the substance to its primordial, purified state—the "red earth" from which the Philosopher's Stone is made.</note>, and I have gone the way of all the world.

<summary>This page describes three allegorical images depicting the "death" and dissolution of a warrior (metal) through fire, salt baths, and the "eagle" (acid), resulting in a "red earth" personified as Adam.</summary>
<keywords>martial allegory, warrior, gallows, eagle, salty bath, rose-colored marrow, red earth, Adam, alchemical dissolution</keywords>

# The Fifth Painting.

Here, a dead man is carried without a bier or a coffin, entirely naked, and laid in his vault beneath the earth upon a gravestone <note>In alchemy, the "death" and burial of the hero represent the stage of *putrefaction* or *calcination*, where the material is reduced to a black, "dead" state.</note>; two women accompany the corpse. The one on the right appears to be a Queen, wearing a silver crown upon her head; beside her is written:
> Alas, how our hero’s bones have dried up. Has his strength entirely vanished? May his blood come upon me and my subjects. <note>original: "Ach wie seynd unsers Helden Bein vertrocknet. Ist dann seine Krafft gantz verschwunden ? Sein Blut komme über mich und meine Unterthanen."</note>

The woman on the left is clothed in purple, mournful and veiled; beside her is written:
> My brother, my brother! Alas, how gladly I would have died for you. I thought you were to gladden the King and redeem our sorrowful Queen.

Following these women is a King of very mournful appearance; it is written beside him:
> My dearest and most constant friend: I shall yet descend into the pits <note>original: "unter die Gruben fahren"; likely referring to the deep vessel or the "underworld" of the chemical furnace.</note> with you.

# The Sixth Painting.

Here stands one like a thief, breaking in; it is written beside him:
> Behold, several days have passed since this hero has been at rest; perhaps he still wears his jewels <term>Kleinodien</term><note>While "Kleinodien" means jewels or treasures, in this context it refers to the concentrated "virtues" or powers of the metallic substance.</note>. But what is this? They must have laid his body elsewhere; here is nothing but the **red sweat**; this is to be sold as a sacred relic <term>Heiligthumb</term>; indeed, it is royal

<summary>The "Hero" (the substance) is buried in an allegorical funeral attended by a Silver Queen and a Purple Woman, signifying the peak of the blackening stage. A "thief" finds the tomb empty of the body but filled with "red sweat," representing the first emergence of the red tincture or Philosopher's Stone.</summary>

Blood; <note>original: "Geblüth"; in alchemy, the "blood" represents the red tincture or the vital essence of the stone.</note> nevertheless, it is—alas—nowadays despised by the many; yet were it found with me, I would be hanged. I will pour it out; the street is covered with snow, it will soon freeze and be swallowed by the snow.

## The Seventh Painting.

Here several people pass by, among whom one picks up the dyed snow; beside this is written: This does not belong on the streets; it should be returned to its previous place of rest and bedchamber, so that it may be clarified <term>clarificirt</term><note>To clarify or "clarify" a substance in this context means to purify it, often through heat or filtration, until it reaches a state of transparency or brilliance.</note> and, through sevenfold saturation <term>Eintranckung</term><note>Known as "imbibition" in alchemy, this is the process of repeatedly feeding a dry substance with liquid—the "blood"—to increase its weight and potency.</note> with this blood of the King’s Crown, become seven times heavier.
